## 1. What a “shielded spend” proves (ZK statement)

We’ll design a *single* canonical shielded pool, with a fixed “join–split” circuit used for all transactions.

Formal-verification completion is tracked by `config/highest-standard-formal-verification-matrix.json` and closed at the inventory level by `Hegemon.Release.HighestStandardCompletionCertificate`. A row may reach 100% formal-surface coverage only when the internal theorem target is proved, the production path is bound by Lean-generated vectors or focused Rust conformance gates, formal-core includes the gate, and any remaining primitive/proof-system/parser/hash/storage/DA/external-bridge/release-infrastructure/native-node/performance obligation is an explicit certificate field. The matrix therefore treats external cryptographic hardness and infrastructure behavior as named assumptions, not as unproved prose or hidden implementation equivalence. It independently reports equal-count mechanized assumption closure, currently `10 / 20` tracks (`50.0%`); an explicitly named open track receives no closure credit. Formal-core requires the fixed matrix path to match an independent 20-track inventory and all 26 fixed theorem identities across the ten closed tracks, including every component theorem in the native reducer/preimage/entropy and digit/Euclidean compound tracks; it requires every evidence path to be an existing repository file and each credited theorem in `config/formal-security-claims.json`. The public active-goal command then asks a standalone Lean runtime auditor to import compiled `Hegemon` constants without contributor plugins or environment extensions, compute the claims-and-closure theorem union's dependencies through `Lean.collectAxioms`, and apply the existing axiom-waiver policy before returning a passing result; the aggregate Lean gate repeats that audit independently. It also runs a direct Rust-owned proposition ratchet: the checker runs `lake build Hegemon` under the pinned toolchain before `lake env lean --stdin` emits the fixed identity set's fully elaborated `pp.all` types, and Rust compares a domain-separated BLAKE3 digest of the pinned toolchain, generated query, and complete output with an independent constant. This makes the check valid in a clean checkout with no pre-existing `.olean` files and makes same-name proposition weakening fail even after editable metadata is renewed. Formal-core separately rejects formal-tree symlinks and compares the deterministic digest of every `formal/lean/**/*.lean` path and byte against a Rust-held constant, closing the transitive model-definition and proof-body gap left by a theorem-type-only fingerprint. Lexical theorem discovery is retained only to bind a credited name to a listed non-generator source file; it cannot authorize a new track or theorem identity. The historical active-goal surface percent remains pinned in `config/active-goal-progress.json`; formal-core recomputes both measurements and validates their distinct meanings. Generated Rust conformance gates that carry release assurance use exact fully qualified test identities with a preflight cardinality check, and the all-16 SmallWood profile contract is repeated in the CI red-team campaign so a deleted regression fails closed instead of running zero tests.

The formal essence layer is `Hegemon.Essence.Core`. New validity and privacy proofs should be phrased against its `LedgerState`, `Action`, `Block`, `ObserverView`, and `Transition` model first, then refined from production parser/admission/replay/storage/publication paths into that model. The essence model now derives nullifier and bridge-replay uniqueness from freshness and prior uniqueness, uses authorization witness records instead of boolean admission flags, tracks per-asset balances and conservation directly, binds bridge safety to receipt metadata, separates local projection privacy from timing/topology/miner-ordering/global privacy assumptions, and makes parser/storage refinement exact-byte and fail-closed. The current essence goal is measured by `config/formal-essence-progress.json`, with generated evidence from `lake exe gen_essence_core_vectors` and the gate `scripts/check_essence_core_vectors.py`. This compresses the proof story without pretending to discharge primitive crypto, deployed STARK/PCS soundness, DA retention, storage durability, local zero-knowledge, or traffic-analysis privacy assumptions.

Native 0.10 launch methods use the native profile/environment surface directly. Local and private devnets start with `hegemon-node --dev` and an isolated base path; public 0.10 testnet migration is a fresh native genesis restart with a versioned genesis/release record, not reuse of the deleted 0.9 JSON chain-spec files or a runtime `--chain` flag.

The Electron app's managed node profile is a local wallet control plane, not a public RPC service. For loopback-only managed nodes, the app starts the native node with `--rpc-methods unsafe` so shielded wallet submissions can call `hegemon_submitAction`; when RPC is exposed externally, the app forces `--rpc-methods safe` and the wallet must use a trusted local control plane instead of direct public RPC. Desktop wallet endpoints must be localhost URLs; no-SSH operation is achieved by running a local P2P relay node, not by pointing the app at a public RPC socket. The shipped Electron shell must be packaged with context isolation, renderer sandboxing, no renderer Node integration, denied popups/webviews/navigation escapes, denied permission prompts, a CSP that keeps script execution on the packaged app, and typed preload IPC for privileged desktop actions such as clipboard writes. Managed desktop mining uses only the miner address saved in the visible connection profile; it must not silently inherit a parent-process `HEGEMON_MINER_ADDRESS`. The app UI guard is an explicit release check: it locks the startup live-node display against transient `target 0` / false mining-gate races, prevents legacy 0.9-era contacts from being selectable in the 0.10 send flow, keeps the effective mining payout address visible beside the wallet receiving address, and prevents renderer clipboard calls or copy-address failures from masquerading as wallet-health failures. Local `npm --prefix hegemon-app run package` builds may be ad-hoc signed for validation, but production macOS artifacts must use `npm --prefix hegemon-app run dist:prod` with Apple notarization credentials so the release fails closed if notarization cannot run.

Native no-SSH transaction propagation depends on P2P pending-action relay, not on opening unsafe RPC to the internet. After local `hegemon_submitAction` validates and durably stages a self-contained user action, the node broadcasts a `PendingAction` sync message containing the canonical SCALE bytes. A peer exact-decodes those bytes, checks the embedded `pending_action_hash`, rejects coinbase and candidate-artifact routes as miner-local, runs the same mempool route/state/byte-budget validation used by local staging, persists accepted actions to `mempool_actions`, and rebroadcasts only newly admitted actions. Non-proof relays enter a bounded fair per-peer queue and call the durable group-commit staging path on at most four blocking workers rather than performing sled transaction/flush work on the Tokio receive loop; accounting covers queued and active work, caps each peer at two outstanding actions, and caps the queue globally at 512 actions and the native mempool byte budget. This path does not consume proof-verification permits, and a relay is broadcast only after durable staging succeeds. Nodes also periodically rebroadcast bounded batches of still-pending peer-relayable actions so a dropped or backpressured relay does not permanently strand a transaction on a non-authoring peer. Duplicate raw hashes and duplicate semantic actions are accepted as no-ops and stop propagation. Focused Rust regressions cover staging/persistence/deduplication, stale-candidate eviction, miner-artifact rejection, hash-binding mismatch, miner-local exclusion from rebroadcast batches, queue item/byte/per-peer caps, fair progress, and receive-loop responsiveness while group-commit flush is held; Lean raw-ingress vectors cover the pending-action sync variant's wire shape and malformed empty relay rejection. This is transaction-propagation implementation evidence, not a proof that honest peers are online or that the network delivers every relay.

The resource-budget integration has explicit refinement boundaries. Local RPC and relayed action staging propagate the saturated mempool byte-budget rejection inside invoked staging closures before grouped persistence. The grouped commit separately evaluates the budget for each candidate before adding it to the accepted set, and the later sled transaction persists only that accepted set. Ciphertext upload parses and prepares individually bounded values, then under the persistence epoch checks staged-count capacity for each prepared ciphertext before projected insertion, persistence, durability flush, and publication. Staged-proof upload checks both projected bytes and staged-count capacity before decoded artifact binding and `prepared_proofs.push`, then rechecks both limits under the persistence epoch before projected insertion and all persistence/publication. Grouped pending-action durability calls the flush helper lexically before each corresponding in-memory insertion. The blueprint binds the exact loop-local callers and lexical orders, while focused byte-budget, pre-heavy rejection, mixed-batch atomicity, coalescing, transaction-failure, flush-failure, and durable-restart tests provide conditional integration evidence. The current static checker deliberately excludes closure-contained calls from direct binding evidence and does not prove closure propagation, loop-to-transaction, cross-loop, or outer batch loop-to-completion control flow, so those refinements remain recorded residuals rather than machine-proved dominance. Graceful shutdown calls the typed database flush before recording completion, but currently logs rather than propagates a flush failure.

Native devnet mining liveness is validated by execution, not by the formal PoW admission kernels. The bootstrap `--dev` target is `GENESIS_BITS` (about 1,000,000 expected hashes per block), chosen to let ordinary CPU miners get past genesis and early blocks before retargeting moves toward the 60-second protocol interval. Runtime miners search batched disjoint nonce ranges per worker and report hash rate from attempted hashes; focused regressions check that accounting path, but production readiness still requires live measurement of observed H/s and block intervals under the release binary. Any release or shared-devnet reset must run `scripts/test-node.sh devnet-liveness` or an equivalent two-node live check that proves both the authoring node and a seeded follower advance beyond height 1. The existing Lean/vector checks cover compact-target arithmetic, retarget scheduling, timestamp admission, work-template admission, and mined/announced-block rejection order; they do not prove seal-search liveness, host hash rate, peer convergence, or operator clock health.

Residual closure work follows `Hegemon.Release.AssumptionClosureRoadmap`, `.agent/RESIDUAL_ASSUMPTION_CLOSURE_EXECPLAN.md`, and the track inventory in `config/highest-standard-formal-verification-matrix.json`. Parser/native/proof/bridge residuals are mechanized-refinement tracks and should be reduced by executable Lean specifications, generated vectors, arbitrary-byte or mutation corpora, and production Rust gates. The scoped raw-ingress, exact-decode, canonical-publication, reorg, and startup row-equivalence family is already closed; arbitrary parser internals and complete native-node equivalence are not. Primitive cryptographic hardness remains named assumption material until an in-repo or externally reviewed reduction is available. DA retention, storage/fsync semantics, global privacy traffic assumptions, release-infrastructure enforcement, dependency scanner completeness, and performance preservation are system-model assumptions and must be made fail-closed through explicit evidence gates and monitoring. `Hegemon.Release.SystemModelAssumptionGate` and `cargo run --manifest-path scripts/hegemon_formal_core/Cargo.toml -- check-system-model-gates config/system-model-assumption-gates.json` are the checked enforcement path for those categories.

Say each transaction supports up to:

* `M` inputs (old notes),
* `N` outputs (new notes),

per proof. Use fixed `M, N` for the base circuit, recursion if you need more.

### 1.1 Data model

A **note** is conceptually:

* `value` - integer (e.g. 64-bit, or 128-bit if you're paranoid)
* `asset_id` - 64-bit label (u64) in the current circuit, encoded as a single field element inside the STARK. Fresh-V3 authoritative note commitments, nullifiers, transaction Merkle hashes, anchors, balance tags, and state roots are distinct typed 48-byte RFC 7693 BLAKE2b-384 outputs under centrally registered domains. Poseidon outputs are legacy/research-only and cannot enter an active V3 statement or header. `0` = the native coin.
* `pk_recipient` – an encoding of the recipient’s “note‑receiving” public data (tied to their incoming viewing key)
* `pk_auth` – a spend-authorization public key derived from the owner’s spend secret. For private predicate or private multisig accumulator custody, this hidden note slot can instead commit to policy or accumulator material; the spend proof must rederive that commitment key from private witness data and match the consumed note opening before authorization continues.
* `rho` – per‑note secret (random)
* `r` – commitment randomness

We define the note commitment:

```text
cm = Com_note(value, asset_id, pk_recipient, pk_auth, rho, r)
   = Hc("note" || enc(value) || asset_id || pk_recipient || rho || r || pk_auth)
```

* `Hc` is a commitment‑strength hash (could be domain‑separated Poseidon or Blake3; binding+hiding rely on hash + randomness).
* `enc(value)` is some fixed‑width encoding for `value`.

Private multisig uses a stateful accumulator authorization layer rather than signatures or MPC. On the current proven branch scope, the hidden policy is a fixed-capacity private `m-of-n` relation with one to six active circuit-derived signer tags, each tag being the five-limb SmallWood spend authorization digest derived from that signer’s spend key. The private policy root is `smallwood_policy_root_bytes(threshold, signer_count, padded_policy_signer_tags)`, where active tags are sorted and padded to six private witness slots. The wallet rejects zero thresholds, thresholds above signer count, duplicate tags, noncanonical tags, inactive nonzero padding, and active first-limb tag collisions; the AIR uses the first limb as the compact distinctness witness for active policy slots. A value-lock transaction first consumes a normal spendable value note and recreates that value under a hidden `pk_auth` derived from `(policy_root, final_intent_digest)`, with the exact final plan stored in the encrypted local opening. An approval consumes a private accumulator/control note and a private signer capability/value note, proves that the approving input’s spend-derived tag is one active hidden policy tag, rejects duplicate approval by hidden approved-slot bits, and produces a new accumulator note for the same exact `(account, policy_root, intent_digest)`. The final spender receives only the value-locked note plus the accumulator note. The final spend witness contains no signer long-term secret; it accepts only when the value note is locked to the exact hidden policy and intent, the private accumulator matches the exact account, policy, and intent digest, and its private approval count reaches the private threshold. The hidden `pk_auth` slot is the value-lock and accumulator commitment key, so the proof cannot select a different private policy, threshold, signer count, approved-slot state, accumulator opening, or final spend intent after the notes are created. The normal public transaction shape stays unchanged: public signer sets, thresholds, signer counts, approval counts, approval leaves, policy roots, approved slots, and approval nullifiers are not transaction fields.

For stateful private accumulator multisig, wallet software treats account creation, value lock, and setup as hidden policy-note construction steps, but value lock and setup are still funded shielded transactions. The value-lock builder consumes the source value note, pays an explicit lock fee, creates the value-locked note for the exact final statement digest, and persists the exact final plan in the encrypted local opening. Setup consumes a normal spendable native note, pays an explicit fee, creates the initial zero-value accumulator/control note for that same digest, and returns native change only if it fits the two-output shape. The encrypted wallet stores the private policy descriptor plus local accumulator/control and value-lock openings; public wallet records expose only opaque identifiers and commitments. Separate signer wallets import the private account descriptor and the current accumulator-note opening over an off-chain channel before building their approval. The final spender imports the value-lock and threshold-accumulator openings, never signer long-term secrets. Local opening reconciliation is accepted only for an exact chain commitment that already appears in the verified wallet commitment list, and the stored opening must recompute that exact commitment. Each signer builds and submits their own shielded approval transaction from their local wallet, so their long-term spend/auth secret never reaches a coordinator. The approval relation privately proves membership by equating the signer note’s spend-derived tag with one active hidden policy tag, consumes the previous exact-intent accumulator note, and creates the next accumulator note with that hidden policy slot marked approved. The final-spend relation consumes the value-locked note and the threshold accumulator note for the same digest; that digest is the SmallWood final public-statement digest, so recipient, amount, fee, asset, root, output commitment, ciphertext hash, or transaction-binding changes cannot reuse the prior approvals. Wallet APIs that would exchange host-verified opaque approval packages remain fail-closed; product flows must use the proof-backed shielded value-lock, setup, approval, and final transaction builders.

On‑chain, the **global state** for the pool is:

* An append‑only **Merkle tree of `cm`** (the note commitment tree)
* A **nullifier set**: any `nf` that has appeared as an input is “spent”

Each **transaction** includes:

* Public:

  * `nf[0..M-1]` – nullifiers for each consumed note
  * `cm'[0..N-1]` – commitments for each new note
  * `ct_hash[0..N-1]` – ciphertext hashes for each output note (domain-separated BLAKE3-384)
  * `balance_tag` – a compressed representation of value balance (see below)
  * optional `memo`s, optional miner tip, etc.
  * one or a few STARK proofs
* Hidden (witness in the proof):

  * openings of the consumed and created notes
  * sender’s secret keys
  * Merkle paths for each input note

### 1.2 ZK statement per transaction

The core statement the STARK proves:

> There exist:
>
> * for each input `i` in `[0..M-1]`:
>
>   * `(value_i, asset_i, pk_recipient_i, pk_auth_i, rho_i, r_i, pos_i)`
>   * `sk_spend`
>   * for predicate threshold notes only: the private policy opening `(policy_root_i, threshold_i, policy_commitment_randomness_i)` whose derived policy commitment key equals `pk_auth_i`
> * for each output `j` in `[0..N-1]`:
>
>   * `(value'_j, asset'_j, pk'_j, pk'_auth_j, rho'_j, r'_j)`
>
> such that:
>
> 1. **Note commitments match**
>
>    * For all inputs/outputs:
>
>      ```text
>      cm_i  = Com_note(value_i,  asset_i,  pk_recipient_i,  pk_auth_i,  rho_i,  r_i)
>      cm'_j = Com_note(value'_j, asset'_j, pk'_j, pk'_auth_j, rho'_j, r'_j)
>      ```
>    * And the published `cm'_j` equal these.
> 2. **Inputs are in the tree (membership)**
>
>    * For each input:
>
>      ```text
>      MerkleRoot == Merkle(cm_i, pos_i, path_i)
>      ```
>
>      where `path_i` is the Merkle authentication path, and `Merkle()` is a hash‑based tree function fixed by the protocol.
>      The executable Merkle path control flow is represented in Lean: generated vectors check sibling-depth admission, position-bit left/right orientation, root folding, and wrong-root rejection against the production `MerklePath` helper. The commitment-tree membership handoff is now separately represented: Lean packages consensus-root/state-root/provided-root agreement, root-history membership, state path export, and transaction Merkle-path acceptance, while formal-core checks production `CommitmentTreeState`, `state_merkle::CommitmentTree`, and transaction `MerklePath::verify_with_depth_and_node` agree under the real `merkle_node` combiner. A focused `state-merkle` oracle independently rebuilds append roots, root history, and authentication paths for the production tree and rejects stale roots, full-tree appends, out-of-range paths, and oversized batch extensions before mutation. Poseidon2 hash security and exhaustive implementation equivalence remain separate cryptographic assumptions.
>      The verifier-facing public-input shape is also represented in Lean: generated vectors check fixed input/output widths, boolean flags, inactive zero padding, active nullifier/commitment nonzero rules, nonempty transaction admission, canonical balance-slot asset ordering, and stablecoin asset presence against `the canonical SmallWood verifier-field validator`. The public/serialized input binding seam is represented in Lean as well: generated vectors check merkle-root, fee, signed value-balance, balance-slot asset, stablecoin policy, oracle, and attestation agreement against `transaction_verifier_inputs_from_parts`. The statement-hash preimage grammar is represented in Lean too: generated vectors check the domain separator, digest padding, little-endian scalar encodings, raw stablecoin flag byte, and signed balance encodings against the shared helper used by transaction receipts and consensus tx-leaf binding; the same vector family now checks `tx-public-inputs-digest-v1` postcard preimage bytes against `transaction_public_inputs_digest_preimage_from_serialized` and `tx-proof-digest-v1` backend/proof-byte preimages against `transaction_proof_digest_preimage_from_parts`. The proof statement-binding transcript is represented in Lean as well: generated vectors check the `binding-hash-v3` message, chunk-domain-separated BLAKE2 preimages, stablecoin fields, balance-slot rows, and padding sentinel distinction against `StarkVerifier::compute_binding_hash`. Transaction proof-wrapper admission is represented in Lean as a separate decision table for exact consumption, canonical re-encode, backend support, proof/public-input presence, verifier-facing public-input validity, wrapper balance-slot agreement, and final verifier acceptance; `tx-proof-manifest` now routes nested wrappers through the same pre-verifier helper and uses the shared statement-hash helper instead of maintaining a duplicate grammar. The native tx-leaf artifact wire grammar is separately represented in Lean: generated vectors check bounded serialized STARK/public-tx counts, proof-length caps, commitment row/coeff caps, permissive backend-byte defaulting at decode, strict explicit-backend-byte admission for release verification, bad backend rejection, trailing-byte rejection, and truncation rejection against production `superneo-hegemon` decoding, canonical decoding, and canonical re-encoding. The native receipt-root artifact parser and structural schedule/resource gate are represented in Lean as well: generated vectors check leaf/fold caps, non-empty expected leaf agreement, `fold_count = leaf_count - 1`, exact fold challenge count, exact parent-row dimensions, trailing-byte rejection, truncation rejection, and raw/decoded/item/max-item/aggregate/work resource projection against production `superneo-hegemon` decoding, canonical re-encoding, structural validation, and decoded-artifact resource projection before backend fold verification. The native backend reference-vector review policy is theorem-backed too: generated vectors check candidate-under-review posture, structural-candidate maturity, security-floor metadata, unique supported case names, expected-error metadata, and required valid/invalid tx-leaf and receipt-root case coverage before the independent Rust reference verifier checks artifact bytes. Fresh package regeneration compares stable decoded transaction fields and each receipt-root leaf's stable receipt identity; stochastic proof and commitment values are normalized only after both Rust verifiers bind the full receipt to that leaf's encoded statement digest. BLAKE2/BLAKE3 cryptographic security, native backend proof soundness, native receipt-root cryptographic fold soundness, and full proof-system soundness remain separate targets.
> 3. **Nullifiers are correct**
>
>    * Derive a nullifier key:
>
>      ```text
>      nk = H("nk" || sk_spend)
>      ```
>    * For each input note:
>
>      ```text
>      nf_i = H("nf" || nk || rho_i || pos_i)
>      ```
>    * And the published `nf_i` match these.
> 4. **Balance is preserved** (per asset)
>
>    * Let’s start with single‑asset to keep it clean:
>
>      ```text
>      sum_i value_i  = sum_j value'_j + fee
>      ```
>    * For MASP, we enforce this per `asset_id` — more on that in a moment.
> 5. **No negative values or overflow**
>
>    * Check `0 <= value_i, value'_j <= 2^64 - 1` (or your chosen bound) by range‑checking in‑circuit.

You can think of this as canonical shielded-note semantics with: no ECC, no Pedersen, no RedDSA — everything is hash‑ or lattice‑based.

---

## 2. MASP value‑balance (multi‑asset) in a STARK

In a MASP, each note carries an `asset_id`; a transaction can involve multiple assets, but you must enforce conservation *per asset*.

There are various ways to do this. A reasonably simple STARK‑friendly approach:

### 2.1 Commit to per‑asset balances inside the circuit

Define:

```text
Δ_k = (total_inputs of asset k) - (total_outputs of asset k)
```

We want:

* For the native asset `k = 0`: `Δ_0 = fee` (or `Δ_0 = issuance + fee`)
* For all other assets `k != 0`: `Δ_k = 0`

Instead of explicitly enumerating all possible assets in the circuit, you:

1. Compute a **multiset of (asset_id, signed_value_delta)** inside the circuit:

   * For each input:   add `(asset_i,  value_i)`
   * For each output:  add `(asset'_j, -value'_j)`

2. Sort this multiset by `asset_id` *inside the circuit* (or enforce a permutation against a sorted copy). This is standard in modern SNARK/STARK design: you pay constraints proportional to `M+N` and log of that for the sort.

3. Aggregate runs with the same `asset_id`:

   * For each run of equal `asset_id = k`, sum the deltas to get `Δ_k`.

4. Check:

   * For the designated “native asset” id (e.g. all‑zero or some constant):

     ```text
     Δ_native = fee + issuance
     ```
   * For all other `k`: `Δ_k = 0`.

5. Output a single public field element `balance_tag` that is, say, a commitment to `(Δ_native, fee, issuance)` — used by nodes for sanity and future audit.

This gives MASP semantics without any ECC:

* The sort + run‑sum works over plain integers in the STARK field.
* The size cost is O((M+N) log (M+N)) constraints, which is manageable at the target M,N.

If you want to be more aggressive, you can avoid exposing per‑asset details publicly: the proof enforces the equalities, but `balance_tag` is simply a commitment to the whole vector `(Δ_k)`. Nodes don’t need to inspect it; they only check that the proof verifies.

### 2.2 Stablecoin issuance binding

Stablecoin issuance and burn are handled as a controlled exception to the per-asset conservation rules. The circuit allows exactly one non-native asset id to have a non-zero delta when a stablecoin binding is present. The binding is part of the public inputs and includes:

* `stablecoin_asset_id`
* `issuance_delta` (signed, exposed as sign + magnitude)
* `policy_hash`
* `oracle_commitment`
* `attestation_commitment`
* `policy_version`

Inside the AIR, the stablecoin binding payload stays in the public inputs. The fixed four balance-slot asset ids are also public inputs, with a canonical encoding enforced by the runtime and wallet: slot `0` is always the native asset, non-native asset ids are strictly increasing, and any `u64::MAX` padding must appear only as a suffix. The witness trace carries only the running in/out sums for those four slots plus a compact 2-bit selector for the chosen non-native balance slot: `00` means “no stablecoin binding,” while `01`, `10`, and `11` select non-native slots `1`, `2`, and `3` respectively. When the binding is enabled, that selected slot must match `stablecoin_asset_id` and its net delta must equal `issuance_delta`; the balance kernel proves `validBalance_stablecoin_padding_field_alias_rejected`, and production `verify_balance_slots` rejects the reserved Goldilocks padding-field alias before any issuance exception is applied. All other non-native slots are still constrained to zero. Wallet/RPC construction obtains the binding from the active `StablecoinPolicy`, fresh oracle commitment, and undisputed attestation through a helper that binds the storage-backed policy hash, oracle commitment, attestation commitment, issuance delta, and policy version into the exact payload while rejecting inactive, ambiguous, stale, disputed, zero-issuance, or over-range records. Node admission then binds the submitted stablecoin payload through public-input, statement, proof-binding, native tx-leaf/action equality checks, and a theorem-backed native manifest authorization table requiring active policy, enabled/retired lifecycle, matching policy hash/version, matching oracle and attestation commitments, undisputed attestation, fresh oracle submission, nonzero issuance, and max-mint/u64 bounds. This keeps issuance fully shielded while still tethering the proof payload to protocol-approved policy inputs; replayed dynamic policy/oracle/attestation state and full native-node `LiveStablecoinPolicyAuthorizes` refinement remain explicit follow-on targets.

Consensus stitches this MASP output into PoW validation with an optional shielded coinbase action. Omitting coinbase forfeits the
subsidy and fees; if a coinbase is present, active V2 requires exactly one, requires it to be the final action, and requires its
amount to equal the checked subsidy plus checked shielded-transfer fees. Multiple or non-final coinbases reject in the early block
policy gate before proof or DA work. Full nodes recompute the running `supply_digest = parent_digest + minted + fees − burns`, and
reject an over-subsidy/reward-mismatched coinbase before fork-choice publication. This keeps the STARK circuit, MASP accounting,
and the PoW header’s supply digest in lockstep while preserving intentional subsidy forfeiture. The executable subsidy schedule is
represented in Lean: named theorems pin the tokenomics constants, capped halving epoch,
zero-height subsidy, first/second halving boundaries, and post-cap extinction, and generated vectors check those facts against
`consensus::reward::block_subsidy`. The executable supply-accounting rule is also represented in Lean: generated vectors check
`CoinbaseData::net_native_delta`, `consensus::reward::update_supply_digest`, and the native node's checked no-coinbase/coinbase
supply helpers, including overflow rejection.
The accepted-chain supply invariant is represented separately in Lean: generated vectors check that every accepted claimed
`supply_digest` equals replaying the executable checked deltas from genesis, while counterfeit claimed supply, underflow, and
overflow are rejected by the production consensus transition helper used before PoW header acceptance. The cross-layer supply
theorems also show accepted native transaction-chain deltas and canonical no-theft/public-field boundary facts feed a valid
claimed-supply step when the consensus fee field is explicitly bound to the authorized native delta; full production fee aggregation
and block-construction equivalence remain separate targets.
The native block coinbase admission table is represented separately in Lean: generated vectors check that blocks have at most one coinbase action, no-coinbase blocks are accepted without summing fees, any single coinbase is final, a present coinbase amount equals the checked subsidy plus checked shielded-transfer fee total, fee/reward overflows reject, and reward mismatches reject.
The native codec-admission boundary is represented separately in Lean: generated schema-v4 vectors check that bounded sync messages reject legacy bincode and trailing bytes, exact SCALE and bincode helpers reject unconsumed bytes plus parser-accepted bytes that do not round-trip to the canonical encoding, native metadata decode chooses current identity-free `NativeBlockMeta` exact bincode while identifying and rejecting retired legacy encodings and rejects trailing bytes in either format, and decoded block actions reject header/action count mismatches or trailing action payloads before semantic validation. Native metadata exact decode also has an independent bincode fixint mutation-oracle gate over valid current and legacy unsigned metadata, trailing bytes, truncations, deterministic noise, byte-budget rejection, and action-count and action-payload overruns; focused regressions separately require explicit rejection of identity-bearing V2 and signed V1 metadata; the oracle separately requires the same pre-decode budget acceptance, complete byte consumption, and canonical re-serialization as the production helper. The packaged non-malleability theorem proves the inverse acceptance direction for the modeled codec surfaces: an accepted sync decode consumed the full bounded wire message, an accepted exact decode consumed all bytes and matched its canonical re-encoding, accepted metadata decode selected the current format, identified every exact legacy decode as forbidden, and failed closed when no exact decoder matched, and an accepted block-action decode matched the declared action count and exact action payloads. The concrete `PendingAction` SCALE wire grammar now has a Lean/vector gate for no-candidate actions and historical recursive-block and bounded receipt-root `CandidateArtifact::Some` wire lanes: generated raw bytes cover valid empty and one-each no-candidate actions, valid recursive-block and receipt-root candidate-artifact Some actions whose `public_args` are live `SubmitCandidateArtifactArgs` bytes matching the duplicated `candidate_artifact`, empty, short, trailing, invalid option tag, noncanonical compact-prefix, vector-count overrun, public-args overrun, truncated candidate-Some, candidate-Some trailing, invalid candidate proof-mode/kind tags, recursive proof-length overrun, and receipt-count overrun, and production checks those bytes through `PendingAction::encode` and `decode_scale_exact::<PendingAction>`. The coinbase, inline-transfer, sidecar-transfer, and bridge routes now have sibling `CoinbaseActionPayloadScaleWire`, `ShieldedTransferInlineScaleWire`, `ShieldedTransferSidecarScaleWire`, `OutboundBridgeActionPayloadScaleWire`, `InboundBridgeActionPayloadScaleWire`, and `BridgeVerifierRegistrationScaleWire` Lean/vector/Rust conformance slices for raw `MintCoinbaseArgs`, bounded `ShieldedTransferInlineArgs`, bounded `ShieldedTransferSidecarArgs`, `OutboundBridgeArgsV1`, `InboundBridgeArgsV1`, and `BridgeVerifierRegistrationV1` SCALE bytes, including fixed field widths, compact-prefix canonicality where dynamic vectors exist, full consumption, and canonical re-encoding; both shielded-transfer wire slices include bounded empty, one-output, and stablecoin-some product lanes. The bounded canonical codec gate packages the PendingAction, MintCoinbaseArgs, ShieldedTransferInlineArgs, ShieldedTransferSidecarArgs, OutboundBridgeArgsV1, InboundBridgeArgsV1, and BridgeVerifierRegistrationV1 wire witnesses, exact-decode adapter equality, metadata ordering, and canonical publication facts on one review surface. The pending/block action parser-resource theorem composes those accepted codec predicates with the reusable bounded-request cap table, pending reload, and wire replay projection so resource item count matches declared, decoded, and wire-row action counts. The live `hegemon_submitAction` projection boundary is represented separately too: generated vectors check strict known JSON fields, wallet-compatible empty kernel-envelope fields, rejection of non-empty unimplemented envelope fields, supported routes, scoped/bounded/valid nullifiers, bounded/base64-decoded `public_args`, and exact route-payload decoding before `PendingAction` construction. Route-specific semantic admission then runs before staging/publishing: bridge requests are converted to zero-delta pending actions and routed through `validate_action_state`, while shielded-transfer, coinbase, and candidate-artifact requests keep their route-local payload checks. Candidate-artifact semantic admission now also checks that the pending action route `public_args` exact-decodes as `SubmitCandidateArtifactArgs` and equals `candidate_artifact`; `route_payload_decode_failed_rejects` and `route_payload_artifact_mismatch_rejects` pin those fail-closed cases in Lean, and `CandidateArtifactScaleWire` now supplies standalone raw-byte witnesses for the `CandidateArtifact` / `SubmitCandidateArtifactArgs` SCALE grammar checked against production exact decode. The native action-hash admission boundary then checks every decoded block action's embedded `tx_hash` against the recomputed `pending_action_hash` and rejects duplicate action hashes before import or decode-only consumers observe the decoded actions. Persisted pending-action reload is represented by a separate Lean-conformance-checked admission table: malformed sled keys, sled-key/embedded-hash drift, recomputed `pending_action_hash` drift, and duplicate loaded hashes reject before reloaded local mempool entries can feed mining selection. The pending-action field projection gate now additionally checks full decoded fields, route payload fields, validation-step fields, materialized/planned payload rows, wire replay rows, and exact canonical index rows for the covered production paths; `PendingActionFieldProjectionVectors` adds Lean-generated sidecar/bridge/candidate/cumulative-index row-order and row-content vectors checked against production canonical index rebuild. These theorems cover the admission decision tables, current projection gates, current-only metadata selection and legacy rejection, the native metadata mutation-oracle gate, and the concrete `PendingAction`/`MintCoinbaseArgs`/`ShieldedTransferInlineArgs`/`ShieldedTransferSidecarArgs`/`OutboundBridgeArgsV1`/`InboundBridgeArgsV1`/`BridgeVerifierRegistrationV1` wire-vector slices, not sled durability, serde/base64/SCALE/bincode parser internals for all arbitrary bytes, arbitrary custom candidate-artifact payload grammars, arbitrary stablecoin policy semantics beyond the covered route wire fixtures, or disk integrity.
The raw `hegemon_submitAction` JSON ingress surface now has a representative byte-vector gate in addition to the decoded predicate vectors: `ActionRequestRawJsonProjection` owns raw request bytes for valid outbound bridge ingress and malformed/unknown-field/envelope/nullifier/base64/trailing-payload rejection cases, and the Rust gate drives those bytes through production serde_json parsing, strict `SubmitActionRpcRequest` decoding, base64 public_args decoding, and exact route-payload admission. The native decoded bridge action payload admission table is represented separately in Lean: generated vectors check bridge-family routing, no state-delta smuggling, supported bridge action IDs, outbound payload presence, inbound proof-receipt presence, inbound replay-key/message binding, Hegemon destination binding, inbound payload-hash binding, and rejection precedence before receipt verification or replay-set import. The bridge action resource slice maps encoded `public_args`, outbound payload bytes, inbound proof-receipt bytes, inbound message-payload bytes, aggregate dynamic bytes, dynamic item count, and payload work units into the reusable bounded-request table. Production uses the same helper after exact bridge payload decode and, for inbound actions, before payload-hash binding, nested inbound receipt decode, or receipt verification, with focused regressions for oversized receipts and message payloads. Verifier-registration actions now also run a Lean/vector-checked registration policy after exact decode: a registration remains mint-inert unless the descriptor matches the release, activation is reached, a PQ-clean verifier is bound, external verifier soundness is accepted, and positive inbound minting is explicitly enabled. Live `hegemon_submitAction` bridge staging uses the same state-validation route: it constructs zero-delta bridge pending actions, runs `validate_action_state`/`validate_pending_action_against_mempool_state`/`validate_bridge_action_payload`, and rejects malformed or over-cap bridge payloads before action hashing, sled insertion, durability flushing, or pending-map publication. The post-verifier inbound receipt-output admission table is represented separately too: generated vectors check source-chain, rules-hash, message-nonce, message-hash, tip/checkpoint arithmetic, native confirmation-width overflow, confirmation overstatement, minimum-confirmation policy, and rejection precedence against the production helper called by `verify_inbound_bridge_receipt` after a receipt output is decoded. After that decoded-output admission, production now threads the mempool or block-validation replay state into the same mint/replay policy helper and keeps mint authorization disabled, so a verified receipt cannot bypass replay freshness, payload binding, or explicit PQ-clean mint authorization. That helper performs consumed-replay duplicate checks without mutating state, rejects disabled mint authorization before fresh replay import, and imports the replay key only on the accepting path after amount/range binding. Startup reload of the consumed inbound replay set is represented by a separate Lean-conformance-checked admission table that rejects malformed persisted keys/markers, requires exact equality with replay-derived canonical block history before mining or serving state, and now exposes loaded consumed replay handoff facts consumed by the raw-ingress bridge publication layer.
The native decoded shielded-transfer payload admission table is represented separately in Lean: generated vectors check proof presence and byte caps, anchor and commitment agreement, inline ciphertext byte caps, ciphertext hash/size metadata agreement, binding-hash agreement, decoded proof binding-hash agreement, fee agreement, and rejection precedence before anchor/nullifier state and tx-leaf proof verification. Accepted payloads now expose binding-hash, proof-binding-hash, fee, and inline resource facts for downstream native tx-leaf theorem composition. The wallet note-ciphertext wire boundary now proves generic chain/DA parse-project agreement for any byte-bounded accepted chain wire, packages the DA ciphertext-hash preimage under an explicit hash-match predicate, and packages accepted decrypt plus that hash boundary with explicit production-profile facts for `wallet-aead`, `note-aead`, `memo-aead`, 32-byte KEM randomness, 32-byte ChaCha20-Poly1305 keys, 12-byte nonces, 7-byte metadata AAD, fixed ML-KEM ciphertext length, and explicit ML-KEM, AEAD, KDF, and RNG residual assumptions. The native wallet ciphertext archive boundary separately proves accepted observer-visible archive rows are contiguous from zero and strictly below `leaf_count`, and now composes those archive facts with the wallet ciphertext production/decrypt/hash boundary, while generated schema-v7 vectors check accepted prefixes, archive gaps, leaf-count overruns, domain/profile constants, chain-to-DA projection, malformed fail-closed wires, DA hash preimages, and decrypt rejections against production wallet and crypto code. Transfer-state admission vectors additionally derive the nullifier summary from concrete spent, pending, and action nullifier rows for both mempool staging and block import, checking the production duplicate precedence before that summary is consumed by the state-admission table.
The native decoded block-action validation composition is represented separately in Lean: generated vectors check that action hash/count admission, route/scope admission, route-specific payload success, inbound bridge replay duplicate checks, shielded transfer ordering, and transfer state admission execute in fixed order before decoded actions are persisted or mutate state. Active V3 removes `PendingAction.received_ms` from the wire grammar entirely at local construction, peer relay, mempool insertion, stored-row reload, block decode/import, startup, and reorg. Exact historical V1 and interim V2 timestamp-bearing rows are identified and rejected rather than upgraded before hashing or persistence. Peer relay first reads the fixed 56-byte active-V3 SCALE discriminator (ActionId48, version binding, family, action) and rejects truncated, inactive, or unknown routes before any length-bearing action-vector decode; only the inline-transfer route proceeds to exact decode and identity validation. Arrival and block-template wall time remain out-of-band; block-header timestamps still use the local clock independently. The raw hash and resampling helpers remain only for historical codec/model compatibility, while defense-in-depth reorg overlap compares exact and semantic identities so a legacy semantic variant cannot be resurrected. Native action-ordering has a schema-v3 Lean/vector gate: accepted shielded-transfer `action_order_key_preimage` is checked as `binding_hash || nullifiers` for inline and sidecar transfers, non-transfer preimages are checked as `non-transfer || family_id || action_id || semantic_hash || nullifiers`, and transfer plus non-transfer order keys stay stable under compatibility-only local arrival-time resampling. Native mineable candidate selection and pending-candidate cleanup are also represented in the Lean mineable-action table: generated schema-v3 vectors check zero-transfer candidate rejection, one/two-transfer candidate matching, unmineable sidecar exclusion from transfer count, canonical-order first matching candidate selection against production `select_mineable_actions`, and candidate-artifact pruning whenever any shielded transfer remains pending. Announced-block import and canonical replay also compare decoded action roots to the block header before payload materialization. Announced-block import runs the block replay/accounting/commitment gate before deeper payload and artifact validation so forged body commitments reject under the Lean-modeled no-counterfeiting order instead of being masked by malformed action payloads.
The native action-plan application admission slice is represented separately in Lean: generated vectors check that planned commitment-start rows have the same length as decoded actions, match the current leaf cursor at each step, and advance under checked u64 arithmetic. Production preview, memory apply, materialized planning, and canonical-index rebuild now propagate this gate before appending commitments, deriving roots, or deriving canonical index rows. Native memory apply also builds the next commitment tree off-state and publishes it only after the whole planned batch appends, with a near-full-tree regression covering commitment overflow before tree/nullifier/staged-marker/pending mutation. The companion native action wire-replay projection slice checks decoded `PendingAction` ciphertext hash/size rows and inbound bridge replay-key projection against materialized planned effects before preview, memory apply, materialized planning, or canonical-index rebuild derives replay rows. `PendingActionByteReplayRowCountBinding` now also defines ordered field-projection rows and canonical row projections for decoded, validation, materialized, planned, wire, and canonical-index surfaces; `pending_action_field_projection_binds_decoded_production_fields` and `accepted_pending_action_raw_bytes_bind_production_field_projection_rows` package accepted exact-decode/projection evidence into one decoded/validation/materialized/planned/wire/canonical production field-row review surface, and `pending_action_raw_byte_projection_requires_canonical_decode` proves that this surface implies accepted exact decode for both pending-action and block-action bytes. The Rust gates check raw action bytes, full action fields, exact canonical row contents, and rejection of trailing action bytes before canonical-index row projection. `CommitmentTreeContentRefinement` now also proves the canonical replay-set content boundary: canonical nullifier rows equal exact ordered decoded nullifiers, and canonical bridge replay rows equal exact ordered planned replay keys at the accepted raw-ingress full-byte publication surface. Parser-internal arbitrary raw SCALE proof remains a separate implementation-equivalence target.
The transaction proof-wrapper admission slice is represented separately in Lean: generated vectors check exact/canonical wrapper admission, SmallWood backend routing, proof/public-input presence, public-input validity, balance-slot agreement, and verifier acceptance ordering. Production `transaction-circuit` wrapper verification calls the admission predicate around decoded-field and SmallWood verifier-result checks, and `tx-proof-manifest` exact-decodes nested proofs before routing verifier rejection through the same table; the retired backend discriminant is rejected. Proof-system soundness and bincode internals remain outside the claim. Successful public/serialized input binding is lifted into field-level public-input facts by `bindPublicInputs_some_implies_verifier_binding_facts` and `validBinding_true_has_verifier_binding_facts`, and the canonical deployed-verifier boundary carries those facts through `canonical_statement_surface_verifier_public_input_binding_facts` and `deployed_soundness_canonical_surface_public_input_verifier_binding_facts`. Consensus TxValidityClaim matching is represented in Lean as a sibling post-verification gate: generated vectors check count, receipt statement/proof/public-input/profile fields, and statement binding anchor/fee/circuit-version equality against the verified artifact claims before provided block claims are trusted by aggregation or recursive block paths. The SmallWood public-statement binding slice pins the verifier-facing public statement to the canonical verifier field vector plus the circuit-version and crypto-suite suffix, with generated vectors checked against production normal and stablecoin candidate profile surfaces. The SmallWood verifier statement projection slice reconstructs the production `PackedStatement` verifier-call surface and checks arithmetization, public values, row/packing/degree metadata, all linear-constraint vectors, auxiliary witness limb count, profile material, transcript bytes, proof-byte presence, and explicit verifier-result handoff against Lean-generated drift cases. The resulting target is the exact canonical SmallWood verifier-field merkle-root, fee, signed value-balance, slot-asset, flag, stablecoin payload, public-statement prefix, version suffix, and final verifier-call projection surface plus exact verified-claim matching; it still does not discharge STARK/AIR/SmallWood soundness, witness extraction, bincode correctness, hash security, or complete verifier/native-node refinement.
The transaction spend-authorization boundary is represented separately in Lean: `Hegemon.Transaction.SpendAuthorization.authorizeInputSlots_lengths_match` proves successful slot authorization forces public input flags, public nullifiers, and private witnesses to have matching lengths, and `transactionSpendAuthorized_witnesses_align_with_public_inputs` lifts that fact through the transaction spend-authorization predicate. The SmallWood balance/public-field boundary is represented separately in Lean: `Hegemon.Transaction.SmallWoodBalanceBoundary.accepted_smallwood_balance_constraints_expose_boundary_facts`, `accepted_smallwood_balance_constraints_authorize_native_delta`, `accepted_smallwood_balance_constraints_authorize_selected_stablecoin_delta`, `accepted_smallwood_balance_constraints_disabled_stablecoin_delta_zero`, and `accepted_smallwood_balance_constraints_nonselected_non_native_zero` name the native-delta, stablecoin-delta, disabled-stablecoin, and non-selected non-native facts that the deployed inline-Merkle SmallWood verifier surface must expose. `noncanonical_zero_value_balance_sign_rejected` and `noncanonical_zero_stablecoin_issuance_sign_rejected` pin canonical signed-zero behavior. Rust compares them against `transaction_public_inputs_digest_preimage_from_serialized` and `transaction_proof_digest_preimage_from_parts` before hashing. The release-disabled theorem family additionally proves that if policy receipt verification reflects `risc0ReleaseVerifierAccepts` and the release verifier is disabled, the receipt-mint policy rejects before replay or mint and no raw-ingress bridge mint-replay policy publication facts can be constructed.
The native sync block replay publication slice is represented in Lean as `Hegemon.Native.SyncBlockReplayPublication`. `accepted_sync_block_response_derives_decoded_rows_match_raw_replay` derives decoded-action-row to raw-replay-row agreement from accepted block-action decode plus accepted action wire-replay projection, `accepted_sync_block_response_binds_raw_canonical_publication_from_decoded_rows` feeds that derived equality into the canonical publication package, and `accepted_sync_block_response_with_resource_bounds_binds_raw_canonical_publication_from_decoded_rows` carries accepted bounded-request item-count facts through the same package. Accepted synced raw block batches therefore expose exact sync/block decode facts, response-count bounds, bounded-request cap facts, accepted ledger/tree replay, commitment-root publication, replayed supply equality, replayed leaf-cursor equality, canonical commitment-plan preconditions, and final nullifier/bridge replay-key uniqueness. The active native sync wire preserves legacy ordinals 0 through 3, assigns exact locator and action-body carrier messages to ordinals 4 through 7, rejects the incompatible stored-metadata-record request and chunk variants at ordinals 8 and 9 before postcard allocation, and assigns ordinal 10 to a bounded `AnnounceTip` request-planning hint that cannot authorize block import or canonical progress. Large block transfer uses `AnnounceLocator` or `ResponseLocators`, then `BlockBodyRequest` and bounded `BlockBodyChunk`; `Hegemon.Native.BlockBodyChunkTransportAdmission` checks schema, chain, rules, total length, canonical chunk count and length, body hash, exact decode, and locator equality before the existing response-import path. `Hegemon.Native.SyncRawIngress` and `Hegemon.Native.SyncBlockChunkAdmission` retain the older metadata-record layout only as conditional evidence; those vectors do not authorize production tags 8 and 9. Production feeds admitted bytes through `decode_sync_message`, checks valid bytes against `encode_sync_message`, and then hands decoded requests to `native_sync_response_range` or decoded responses to `admit_and_sort_native_sync_response_blocks` plus `NativeSyncResponseImportProgress`; accepted exact action-body completions enter that same authorized response-import path. `Hegemon.Native.SyncBlockRangePublicationAdmission` now adds the outbound publication decision table for live `block_range`: generated vectors check admitted range, served-count, first/last-height, height-continuity, previous-parent anchor, parent-continuity, canonical-row verification, action-body verification, and rejection precedence against the production helper called before returning peer-visible rows. `Hegemon.Native.SyncResponseImport` covers the inbound response import fold: response-count admission before sort/import, sorted-height projection, attempted/imported/stored-noncanonical counters bounded by the response, stop-on-first-error and stop-on-missing-parent behavior, impossible outcome-trace rejection, and progress-aware continuation. Missing-parent and fully consumed zero-canonical-progress responses block direct continuation; when the local tip is no higher than the peer target, including equal-height fork discovery, their follow-up is admitted only with a changed well-formed recovery range or an explicit deferred backoff, while error stops require backoff. Production `native_sync_loop` routes responses through `admit_and_sort_native_sync_response_blocks` before `NativeSyncResponseImportProgress` and `import_announced_block`, and formal-core checks Lean-generated import and recovery-plan vectors against those helpers. The formal-core gate also runs `block_range_projects_decoded_materialized_wire_rows`, which serves a canonical sync range through production `block_range` and checks the served action bytes derive the same decoded/materialized/planned/wire/canonical projection rows, plus focused sync raw-ingress, response-count/import/publication regressions that prove production maps response count to the generic bounded-request item-count gate before sorting/import and rejects truncated, unanchored, or unverified outbound block ranges before publication. This narrows the sync-path implementation-equivalence gap with cheap raw-byte and control-flow checks; it still does not prove arbitrary postcard or sync/block parser internals, complete fork discovery, production construction of every wire projection from raw bytes, peer honesty, network liveness, proof soundness, storage durability, allocator behavior, or complete native-node equivalence.
The raw-ingress pending-action publication slice is represented in Lean as `Hegemon.Native.RawIngressPendingActionPublicationRefinement`. `accepted_raw_ingress_pending_action_bytes_bind_publication` composes `RawIngressLedgerTreePublicationFacts` with `PendingActionBytePublicationFacts` over the same raw replay witness, so parser row-count agreement, sidecar availability and size agreement, DA-root binding, accepted ledger/tree replay, commitment-root publication, replayed supply, replayed leaf cursor, final nullifier uniqueness, and final bridge replay-key uniqueness are exposed together. `accepted_raw_ingress_pending_action_bytes_bind_tx_leaf_publication` adds the native tx-leaf/canonical statement artifact package on the same surface. This still does not prove arbitrary-byte parser internals, DA availability or retention, hash security, proof soundness, sled durability, or complete native-node equivalence.
The raw-ingress full-byte review slices are represented in Lean as `Hegemon.Native.RawIngressActionHashTxLeafPublication`, `Hegemon.Native.RawIngressDaSidecarCanonicalPublication`, `Hegemon.Native.RawIngressFullBytePublicationSurface`, and `Hegemon.Native.NativePublicationRowEquivalence`. These packages project the nested raw-ingress theorem stack into top-level records: action-count/hash/uniqueness fields, statement preimage, binding-message, public-input binding, wrapper facts, value/stablecoin binding, tx-leaf preconditions, native artifact equality facts, proof metadata, proof-byte bounds, DA-root binding, raw/canonical replay, commitment-root publication, replayed supply, final replay-set uniqueness, exact parser facts, wire replay facts, bounded artifact shape, explicit parser-to-canonical projection assumptions, and full statement artifact facts. `accepted_raw_ingress_full_byte_publication_surface_binds_production_projection_rows` composes that surface with accepted block-action validation and named production projection facts, proving decoded payload rows, validated action rows, materialized action rows, materialized payload rows, and wire replay rows agree at the full-byte boundary. `accepted_raw_ingress_full_byte_publication_surface_binds_field_projection_rows` lifts ordered decoded/validation/materialized/planned/wire/canonical field-row equality through the same raw-ingress boundary, and `accepted_mined_block_commit_binds_native_publication_rows` composes that row package with accepted mined-block commit publication facts. The formal-core gate now includes Rust regressions from `NativeBlockMeta.action_bytes` through exact field decode, route payload projection, validation-step projection, sidecar materialization, effect planning, wire replay projection, exact canonical row construction, mined coinbase atomic commit rows, canonical rebuild rows, and startup replay rows. Production mined commit also rejects an exact-decodable substituted action body before sled mutation. The same gate set now includes a combined restarted side-branch regression where a sidecar transfer nullifier and disabled inbound bridge replay key reject before block, height, nullifier, ciphertext-index, archive, or bridge-replay publication rows change; because inbound bridge verification is disabled in the release node, this is fail-closed evidence rather than positive bridge mint publication. This adds one cheap pre-transaction action-byte equality check and otherwise remains cfg(test)/formal metadata; it still does not prove arbitrary-byte parser internals, hash/security equivalence, DA availability, proof soundness, storage durability, positive inbound bridge mint publication, or complete native-node equivalence.
`Hegemon.Native.AcceptedBlockAdmissionSafety.accepted_raw_ingress_full_byte_publication_yields_accepted_block_admission_safety` packages the same accepted full-byte surface into one admission-safety certificate covering exact decode, sidecar/DA binding, statement/root binding, ledger/tree replay, commitment-root publication, replayed supply/leaf cursor, final replay-set uniqueness, and tx-leaf artifact facts. `Hegemon.Native.NativePublicationRowEquivalence.accepted_native_publication_path_family_binds_native_publication_rows` now packages raw ingress, mined commit, announced import, sync import, reorg replay, canonical-index rebuild, `block_range`, and startup repair row-equivalence facts into one path-family theorem. `Hegemon.Native.CommitmentTreeContentRefinement.native_publication_rows_equivalence_binds_commitment_replay_content` derives exact ordered commitment values/indexes, nullifier rows, and bridge replay keys from any native publication row-equivalence package, `native_publication_rows_equivalence_binds_ciphertext_content` derives exact ciphertext index/archive rows, and the path-family theorems lift both content packages through the full raw-ingress/mined/announced/sync/reorg/rebuild/block_range/startup family. `Hegemon.Native.CommitmentTreeContentRefinement.raw_ingress_full_byte_field_projection_binds_ledger_integrity_publication` now packages the same raw-ingress field projection with accepted ledger/tree replay, replayed supply, commitment-root and leaf-cursor publication, final nullifier and bridge replay uniqueness, exact canonical commitment/nullifier/bridge rows, ordered decoded content, and append-summary index/count agreement over one witness. This is the current strongest no-counterfeiting/no-double-spend/commitment-publication certificate at the raw-ingress boundary; it still depends on the explicit parser, hash/security, proof-system, storage-durability, and complete native-node refinement assumptions.
The materialized sidecar DA-blob publication slice is represented in Lean as `Hegemon.Native.MaterializedSidecarDaBlobPublication`. `accepted_materialized_sidecar_da_blob_publication` consumes `RawIngressFullBytePublicationFacts` and exposes materialized sidecar rows, wire replay DA-row binding, candidate DA-root binding, proven-batch DA-root binding, recursive semantic DA-root sourcing, native tx-leaf ciphertext hash and payload-hash binding, statement ciphertext-vector binding, accepted ledger/tree replay, commitment-root publication, replayed supply, and final replay-set uniqueness together. `materialized_sidecar_da_blob_publication_binds_production_projection_rows` now composes that materialized publication surface with accepted block-action validation and named production projection facts, so decoded payload rows, validated action rows, materialized action/payload rows, and wire replay rows remain tied after sidecar publication is materialized. The formal-core gate also runs `canonical_index_rebuild_projects_decoded_materialized_wire_rows`, which checks a multi-block canonical-index rebuild derives exact commitment, nullifier, ciphertext index/archive, and bridge replay rows from the same decoded/materialized/planned/wire action stream; `block_range_projects_decoded_materialized_wire_rows` applies the same exact-row check to served sync ranges; `mined_commit_startup_replay_matches_canonical_publication_rows` checks the mined coinbase commit/startup path; and `mined_commit_rejects_meta_action_bytes_not_matching_planned_actions` checks exact-decodable mined action-byte drift rejection. Production canonical replay, artifact verification, memory replay, reorg replay, and startup canonical-index repair now use the same archive-aware sidecar materializer: after mined commit removes staged DA rows, committed sidecar bytes can be recovered from the canonical archive by commitment index and are still checked against the action ciphertext hash and declared size. The regressions `committed_sidecar_replay_materializes_ciphertext_from_archive` and `startup_rejects_committed_sidecar_archive_hash_drift` gate that fix, including the reopen path. It keeps materialized-row-to-`Transaction::new` equivalence, `Transaction::new`-to-consensus-DA-blob equivalence, DA-root hash/security equivalence, DA availability, proof-system soundness, storage durability below sled, and complete native-node equivalence explicit.
The materialized consensus DA-blob refinement slice is represented in Lean as `Hegemon.Native.MaterializedConsensusDaBlobRefinement`. `accepted_materialized_transfer_payloads_feed_concrete_consensus_da_blob` consumes `MaterializedSidecarDaBlobPublicationFacts` instantiated with concrete `MaterializedRowsFeedTransactionNew` and proves the `transactions.map consensusDaPayload` to `Consensus.DaRoot.daBlob` relation internally, then packages ordered action/payload/transaction binding, consensus DA payload binding, exact `Consensus.DaRoot.daBlob` bytes, explicit u32 transaction/ciphertext length bounds, sidecar materialization facts, DA-root publication facts, tx-leaf ciphertext binding, accepted ledger/tree replay, replayed supply, commitment-root publication, and final replay-set uniqueness. `accepted_materialized_transfer_projection_rows_feed_concrete_consensus_da_blob` now derives that transfer-filtered transaction row package from `RawIngressFullByteProductionProjectionFacts`, so decoded, validated, wire-replay, materialized, and consensus transaction rows remain tied before the concrete DA blob theorem is applied. `materialized_consensus_da_blob_refinement_separates_bridge_replay_rows` packages exact DA blob bytes with final bridge replay uniqueness while proving the projected bridge replay-row count and bridge replay rows are excluded from the DA blob expression. `materialized_consensus_da_blob_refinement_exposes_transfer_filtered_bridge_replay_separation` now carries the filtered consensus transaction rows, projected bridge replay rows, projected replay-row count, exact DA blob bytes, and final bridge replay uniqueness together; The formal-core gate also runs `materialized_sidecar_transfer_payload_builds_consensus_da_blob`, `materialized_sidecar_da_blob_excludes_inbound_bridge_replay_rows`, and `materialized_sidecar_da_blob_bridge_first_excludes_replay_rows`; together they check the production sidecar transfer path through sidecar materialization, consensus transaction construction, DA blob bytes, DA root computation, and mixed sidecar/inbound-bridge replay projection where the inbound replay key is present but does not enter the consensus DA blob in either transfer-first or bridge-first order. The canonical verifier boundary retains split proof-system theorems for compatibility, while the retained V4 exact-map theorem proves generated constraint evaluation implies `ProductionSmallWoodSemanticConstraintsSatisfied`: spend, output, and balance equation families over one generated witness. The generated all-16-mask tables, critical-binding indices, wrapper distinctions, and V4 runtime contract are useful regression evidence, but finite vectors do not prove universal semantic or compiled-machine refinement. No V2/V3/V4 wrapper is production-authorized, and these V4/Poseidon theorems describe neither the rejected V6/HGF6 fixtures nor the fresh test-only `HX448C02` diagnostic or any future successor.
