The `formal-core` implementation-binding checker preserves Rust byte offsets while removing comments and every normal/raw string literal, treats non-ASCII bytes conservatively at identifier boundaries, and rejects macro-token, caller-parameter-shadowed, ordinary deferred-closure, unpolled-async, and statically dead short-circuit evidence. Dominance guards receive the stronger rule that no short-circuit RHS can establish order. Focused unit fixtures must cover every rejected form plus direct calls and the exact consumed `filter(...is_ok())` control before blueprint review digests are renewed; production `Iterator::any` and `Vec::retain` binding sites use explicit loops rather than adding method-name closure exceptions.

Operator-scenario harnesses such as `./scripts/test-node.sh two-node-restart` remain available for manual debugging, with `two-node-restart` included in the native acceptance gate.
Benchmark, simulator, and profiling harnesses such as the release-mode SmallWood candidate benchmark, `wallet-bench`, `go test ./...` in `consensus/bench`, and `netbench` are also manual, not part of default CI.

All jobs operate on Ubuntu runners with Rust stable and the native build dependencies installed via `apt`. Adding new languages or toolchains to the blocking gate requires updating this table, the workflow, and `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md`.

## 7. Node, wallet, and UI operations

Follow [runbooks/miner_wallet_quickstart.md](runbooks/miner_wallet_quickstart.md) whenever you need a reproducible demo:

1. Launch the native `hegemon-node` binary with `HEGEMON_MINE=1` and `--dev` for development block times. The node exposes JSON-RPC on port 9944 and P2P on port 30333 by default. Run `make node` to build. Use `HEGEMON_SEEDS="hegemon.pauli.group:30333,devnet.hegemonprotocol.com:30333"` when joining the shared public fresh testnet, keep every miner on the same approved seed list to avoid forks, and keep NTP/chrony enabled because future-skewed PoW timestamps are rejected.
2. Connect the desktop app or native JSON-RPC tooling to the node RPC endpoint to view live telemetry. When using the desktop app, prefer a persistent base path (avoid `--tmp`), and set `--listen-addr 0.0.0.0:30333` only when you intend to accept IPv4 peer traffic. Expose RPC externally only on trusted networks.
   The desktop app is organized into Overview, Node, Wallet, Send, Disclosure, and Console workspaces. Its global status bar always shows the active node, wallet store, and genesis hash so operators can detect mismatches before sending or mining.
   Use `hegemon_peerGraph` behind unsafe local/operator RPC to retrieve connected peer details plus reported peers (address, direction, best height/hash). Public safe RPC should use aggregate `system_health` peer counts only; `system_peers`, `hegemon_peerList`, and `hegemon_peerGraph` are topology surfaces and are not advertised or served under safe policy.
3. For multi-node setups, start additional nodes with the same `HEGEMON_SEEDS` list (for local testing, `HEGEMON_SEEDS=127.0.0.1:30333` for the first node endpoint).

### Security assurance workflow

#### Current transaction-proof method: no qualifier; diagnostic architecture tournament

The accepted production proof-size frontier is empty. No legacy SmallWood/Poseidon binding, HGF6 candidate, or standalone Binius/M4 artifact is authorized for shielded-transfer validity. The current fail-closed implementation candidate is HGV8RP03/SMZ9 with a 122,863-byte maximum proof projection; its production capability is `None`. `HGF6ST01`, `HGF6ST02`, `HGF6HR02`, `HGR6RM02`, and `HGV6PB02` are permanently rejected historical candidate identities and may never be reinterpreted. No candidate query count or proof-byte cap becomes production authority until the source-bound release evidence passes.

The historical 2-input/2-output, all-16-mask, stablecoin, and all-authorization-mode scalar diagnostic inventory used test-only `HX448C02` grammar 2. Its canonical statement is exactly 869 bytes: 125 little-endian seven-byte Goldilocks limbs for the scalar side and 109 little-endian eight-byte M4 words. All non-stablecoin semantic digests remain 56 bytes, while policy, oracle, and attestation are three direct 48-byte live-compatible fields. The scalar retains a whole caller-supplied `ProtocolManifest` plus current height, mirrors the native existential plausible-entry search, and independently encodes the exact 61-byte SCALE policy tuple before framed native-output-length RFC 7693 BLAKE2b-384 under `hegemon.kernel.stablecoin-policy.v2`; the fixed KAT is `4e36d2e5728b9b3a1eb473aac318800434bf3947817410a281d04e8ea6b68ed133bc48c3570db5c3935126aa76be2100`. It reruns lifecycle, freshness, dispute, issuance, and cap checks during scalar verification, but it is not the selected HGV8RP03 relation.

The same-source M4 lowering consumes each stablecoin authority as six direct words and now emits 20 local non-hash groups, seven transaction hash-link groups, and nine consensus-state groups over 109 statement plus 50 public state words. Source checks cover all masks and modes, ranges, authorization transitions, native and non-native balance, mint/burn, disabled-state uniqueness, equal nonzero expected/provided manifest commitments, equal expected/provided height, selected policy fields, lifecycle, oracle freshness, dispute status, and the full `u128` cap. Sixteen counterfeit mutations target M4 rejection. This is still live-policy compatibility only: whole-manifest BLAKE2b-384 recomputation, selected-entry membership, policy-identity recomputation, and consensus authentication of the expected root/height are four explicit host-only predicates; the current kernel global root excludes the new inactive 14-field commitment; and the source manifest has no active policy. Aggregate scalar-to-M4 execution, compiled/DCE geometry, complete ZK, composed QROM, and retained proof bytes are absent. The three opaque 384-bit authorities alone have a unioned generic quantum-collision ceiling of about 126.415 bits before any PCS/IOP/Fiat--Shamir/hash/grinding loss, so strict stablecoin PQ authority is false. `HX448C01` is retired; `winner=None`, and `HX448C02` has no envelope, network, consensus, or production route.

The bounded architecture tournament also selected no competitor. LaZer Pack is nominally smaller at the paper-headline level, but is not the same relation or a retained artifact; its located security argument is classical-ROM/heuristic, and a favorable finite-QROM measure-and-reprogram composition still requires `lambda >= 261` and `log2|C| >= 260` before a missing quantum interactive-PoK bridge. CFW26 HVZK-WHIR is the strongest theorem-level complete-ZK challenger, but its ready-made R1CS route requires odd characteristic and its printed Construction 11.4 is internally inconsistent: Steps 3 and 8 use coefficient one on each inner mask, the displayed decomposition and value-claim proof use coefficient two, and Step 9 supplies a state/scalar pair to the one-state identity form rather than Definition 5.4's scalar wrapper. No official erratum selects a repair. Merged Plonky3 supplies a hiding PCS and explicitly leaves that Section 11 reduction out of scope; it does not provide Hegemon's full relation reduction, canonical conventional-hash wire, or verifier refinement. The role-by-role escape checker finds the first byte-aligned idealized collision width at 400 bits; its compatibility-preserving `HXQ400A1` experiment is exactly 953 statement bytes, 137 seven-byte limbs, 204 SHA-512 compressions before stablecoin constructors, and 512 secret-key bytes. Its exact semantic slice `648*(2^64+1)^3/2^400 + 15/2^159` is conditional on a concrete tagged-SHA-512 QRO assumption and is not a full composition. Oracle/attestation grammars, concrete-hash loss, proof-leaf hiding, PCS/IOP, Fiat--Shamir, history, complete ZK, and refinement remain null, not zero; all 55 dependency-free tests pass with every authority flag false. Release admission is separately fail-closed: the exact `v*` tag-push workflow is byte-pinned by SHA-512 and its first repository command independently checks that pin before exact-invoking the empty-registry successor gate with `--require-authorized`; the isolated workflow-policy and both authorization regression suites follow in the same dependency-root job, and downstream security, build, and draft-publication jobs depend on that result. Those fixtures and policy mutations test gate mechanics rather than candidate semantics.

The direct SmallWood Boolean adapter is disqualified from the smallest-proof lane. Its source-linked hash-only lowering requires at least 1,258,569 engine rows and projects to about 75.6 MB under the widened current wire formula, which also exceeds the live `u16` grammar. Retained SmallWood separately has a concrete complete-ZK witness-recovery failure. Historical 79-call/124-permutation and 79-call/145-permutation HGF6 relations, 87--118 KB SmallWood samples, and the 1,344,828-byte repeated-B128 M4 artifact remain rejected evidence only.

The current mixed B128/E384 PCS research path is also fail-closed. Its encoder commits interleaved `RS(message)` and `RS(mask)` lanes, and an opening serializes both scalars. For two witnesses satisfying one public Boolean relation, the opened-message supports are disjoint and conditional total-variation distance is exactly one; a public-only simulator is at least one half from one real view. The exact depth-20 coordinate-hit probability is `319 / 2^20`. Opening only a single masked share is the TV-zero control, while reopening its mask restores TV one. More masks, independent SHA-512 tapes, E384, and E512 do not repair an exposed message lane. At `q=319`, the retained E384 maximum-shape serializer projection plus named direct-mask floor is 1,579,520 bytes. A qualifying replacement must commit randomized codewords and keep every opened share witness-independent. CFW26 HVZK-WHIR remains the strongest separate odd-field research route rather than an incremental M4/BaseFold patch, but its printed R1CS reduction cannot carry theorem authority until the ambiguity above is authoritatively corrected or independently reproved and refined.

The composed QROM checker remains false. The best one-level zero-ZK-cost local-policy source screens are E384 at `q=130`, 206,992 bytes, and E512 at `q=130`, 232,768 bytes; these are conditional serializer arithmetic, not proof parameters. The direct BCS statistical-ZK expression gives only 114/115 bits at lambda 512 for the respective committed-unit floors. Strict 128 requires integer lambda 569/565, byte-aligned to 576/568, and then complete geometry recomputation. CMS and Block state big-O QROM losses rather than the repository's exact local constants, and the exact augmented query overhead, base-game arity, IOP/RBR premise, conventional-hash instantiation, grinding, abort, semantic/action/consensus unions, and parser/refinement terms remain absent. `q=318` is only the exact without-replacement count for a separate historical 264-bit component screen; `q=310` is only an incomplete modeled union. Neither is a production recommendation. Routing remains fail-closed until one fresh identity binds an exact compiled relation, complete-ZK construction, composed security certificate above 128 bits, exact verifier/consensus refinement, retained unchanged artifacts, measured bytes, and a source-bound release manifest.

- Release artifact publication uses `scripts/release_artifact_manifest.py` in four stages: create a source-provenance and exact-binary manifest; verify the three canonical release binaries; package the exact bytes read from stable opened handles into a new exclusive per-platform directory; and, in the write-authorized release job, verify and assemble all downloaded bundles. Descriptor-relative `O_NOFOLLOW` traversal is used where available. The portable path rejects symlink/reparse components, compares pre-open metadata with the opened file identity, and still hashes and copies only from that bound descriptor. Package and assembly tests exercise symlink rejection, the portable fallback, exact file-set enforcement, and post-package tamper rejection. The workflow policy checker requires one unconditional executable command for every required verify, binary-audit, package, and assembly step; shell control operators, dead conditions, comments, help/version invocations, reordered or omitted arguments, and missing duplicate `--binary` operands reject.
- Native review vectors use their initial schema. Generation mutates the SmallWood proof bytes and rebinds the canonical receipt digest before recording the invalid-proof case, and the policy checker requires the resulting proof-verification failure rather than a receipt short circuit. Receipt-root contexts contain exact child artifact bytes plus SHA-256 and complete transaction context. Both `superneo-bench` and `native-backend-ref` verify those full child artifacts before rebuilding the fold root. Clean-room package regeneration independently verifies each nondeterministic proof artifact, then compares the complete deterministic transaction, public-input, backend, and commitment-row context for every top-level case and receipt-root child. The fixed 11-case valid/invalid polarity is unchanged.
- The retained SmallWood block-composition vectors use their initial schema and carry an explicit V4 theorem-scope table. Lean's positive V4 row and negative legacy/wrong-suite rows are conformance evidence for that historical model, not production authorization and not a successor identity table. No comparison with `DEFAULT_VERSION_BINDING` or `kernel_manifest().binding_allowed` may activate any new route without a fresh release manifest and all proof gates.
- Follow `docs/SECURITY_REVIEWS.md` whenever commissioning cryptanalysis or third-party audits. Every finding recorded there must reference the code path touched plus the mitigation PR.
- `bash scripts/check_formal_core.sh` is the mandatory formal-core gate for release branches. The claims ledger now contains 121 theorem-backed claims plus one approved tombstone, with 2745 named Lean theorem declarations, 107 production-eligible claims, and 65 explicit residual risks; the blueprint contains 121 nodes, 519 dependency edges, and 682 falsification cases. Every `lean_theorem` claim must list explicit fully qualified `lean_theorems`, and the formal-core checker resolves each symbol against a non-comment declaration in a non-generator Lean evidence file so generated vectors, prose, or theorem-looking text inside Lean comments cannot back an exported theorem claim. The Lean proof gate also imports compiled constants through a standalone runtime environment with extensions and plugins disabled, calls `Lean.collectAxioms` for every claimed theorem, consumes strict JSON records, and requires every dependency to be either an explicitly allowed Lean kernel axiom or a capped temporary family in `config/lean-axiom-waivers.json`; no temporary local or synthetic axiom dependencies are permitted in theorem-backed claims. The blueprint DAG is a JSON review map for that ledger: it makes dependencies, target review, implementation bindings, cheap falsification cases, Lean theorem evidence, axiom policy, and scope boundaries machine-checkable. Source-bound review hashing rejects symlinks, non-regular files, repository escapes, and files above the fixed byte caps before reading. Rust module inventories are parsed with `syn`, recursively resolve exactly one ordinary `name.rs` or `name/mod.rs` file for each production module, exclude only exact `#[cfg(test)]` modules, reject `include!` source injection, `path`, `cfg_attr`, and other production `cfg` selection, enforce per-file and aggregate bounds, and feed both semantic analysis and the version-2 review digest. A declared implementation binding means the checker parses the referenced Rust source, sanitizes ordinary/raw byte and C strings, requires the named callee plus every listed caller to exist outside `cfg`-gated non-production code, rejects duplicate matching free functions or same-impl methods for the bound callee, requires every matching caller body to contain a statically live actual call expression for that callee, rejects required caller bodies that locally shadow the callee through a `let`, `for`, nested `fn`, callable `const` or `static`, tuple-struct constructors, `use`, or closure-parameter binding before call scanning, and rejects arbitrary receiver/path calls for bare bound callees except same-impl `self.callee(...)` or `Self::callee(...)` when that method exists outside tests. Caller selectors may be bare Rust function names or method-qualified `TypeName::method_name` selectors, with qualified selectors matching methods inside inherent impls and trait impls for the named type; bare selectors match every same-named non-test function or method and every match must satisfy the binding. Ordered successor selectors may be bare identifiers or conservative path/member selectors such as `RwLock::new` or `self.persist_block`, with qualified selectors requiring the exact receiver/type path immediately before the call. Bindings can require propagated fallible results with `result_obligation = "must_propagate_result"` using `?` on the direct helper result, including turbofish calls, or an allowed simple `.map(...)`/`.map_err(...)`/`.context(...)`/`.with_context(...)` chain, an explicit `return helper(...).map_err(...);`, or an outer function-body tail return; error-swallowing combinators do not satisfy propagation. Explicit fail-closed handling with `result_obligation = "must_check_result_fail_closed"` recognizes only branches that hit a syntactically rejecting top-level return: empty `return`, `None`, `false`, `Err(...)`, `Err { ... }`, `ControlFlow::Break(...)`, `Ok(None)`, `Ok(false)`, or an exact two-component `(trace_identifier, Err(...))` result tuple; success-shaped returns such as `Ok(accepted)` and constructor recovery chains such as `Err(rejection).unwrap_or(accepted)` reject the binding, including for direct or assignment-bound `match` Err arms and immediately bound `if let Err` checks; `NativeNode::submit_action` uses the separate `must_return_submit_action_rejection` obligation, which accepts only an Err arm returning the exact `serde_json::json!` rejection object and rejects bare or locally shadowed macro names, duplicate or extra fields, non-null hashes, and success-shaped fields; direct loop-skip `if let Err(_) = helper(...) { ... continue; }` checks or direct `match helper(...) { Err(_) => ... continue, ... }` arms can require `result_obligation = "must_check_result_loop_skip_fail_closed"`; iterator filters can require `result_obligation = "must_filter_ok_result"` so direct `.is_ok()` is the returned predicate and the filtered iterator feeds a terminal consumer whose result is returned, explicitly returned, or bound to a non-underscore local whose exact binding reaches the final block sink without rebinding or assignment, and arbitrary `.map(...)` chains are not treated as preserving the filtered set; `select_mineable_actions` uses `must_filter_ok_result`: its direct admission filter produces the final action set, and the admission helper rejects every candidate artifact; tuple wrappers can require `result_obligation = "must_return_tuple_result_component"` so the second result component is tail-returned; native mining-template supply fallback can require `result_obligation = "must_reset_work_template_on_err"` so rejected pending-action supply advancement resets to an empty parent-root template before header construction; boolean predicates can additionally require `result_obligation = "must_guard_false_fail_closed"` for `if !helper(...) { return ... }` guards that top-level terminal-return before successor work; native replay expected-supply reconstruction can require `must_match_some_or_return_supply_delta_invalid`; generic PoW checked supply claims can require `must_match_some_and_compare_supply_claim`. Every call site for the bound helper in the required caller must satisfy the declared obligation. Closure-contained calls cannot satisfy result obligations other than the explicitly consumed `must_filter_ok_result` and `must_contribute_to_mineable_selection` forms, and cannot satisfy dominance, because the textual checker cannot prove deferred closure execution. When a binding declares call-order constraints, the checker requires the Lean-conformance helper call to occur before every declared successor call in the non-test caller body; constraints marked `must_dominate_successors` additionally require the helper call to sit in an earlier statement in the same Rust block or an earlier ancestor-block statement than every successor call, so sibling-branch call references do not satisfy mutation/persistence ordering gates. To make theorem-indexing more precise over time, the blueprint policy now also caps broad order constraints whose theorem list exactly equals their parent claim's full theorem set at 129 constraints and 2412 theorem refs; future work must lower those budgets as per-gate theorem subsets replace broad copies. The current blueprint uses 174 result obligations, 123 dominance constraints covering 289 dominance edges, and 145 call-order constraints covering 337 order edges.
- The dedicated `must_propagate_shared_sled_transaction_result` binding is the sole additional transaction-closure exception. Before checking it, the source gate uses locked offline Cargo metadata to select the unique `hegemon-node` library and operator binary, follows the ordinary attribute-free library-to-`native`-to-`node_impl` module chain, requires a unique public nongeneric `NativeNode` with exactly eleven private `sled::Tree` fields, and pins the exact synchronous mined and canonical-suffix method signatures and contextual planners. The binding then accepts exactly one helper call as the first direct `...?;` statement inside a module-local external-crate-pinned sled UFCS call over the ordered eleven-tree canonical tuple, with the outer transaction result propagated. The helper body is itself pinned: its `Some` arm first calls the rooted typed raw apply and only then yields actual count one; its `None` arm yields zero; it stores the observed count in the manifest, invokes the rooted `super::block_flow` evaluator, and returns the actual count. The selected evaluator, its fourteen count helpers, and raw apply are macro- and compiler-selection-free, use rooted `Result` variants, and feed one ordered parsed-AST digest; mutations for `cfg!`, `option_env!`, external profile constants, unconditional success, semantic helper substitution, bare-result shadowing, and enclosing-impl cfg all reject. Other mutations reject crate-root alias substitution, cfg-dead or nested pins, wrapper or incomplete receivers, reordered closure bindings, wrong helper tree/plan/manifest/context arguments, conditional or post-write calls, duplicate or outside-transaction calls, nested/deferred closures, ignored inner or outer results, recovery, alternate parent structs, and cfg-selected tree fields. Mined and active suffix-reorg commits use this form before every other shared-tree mutation. This is a count-only closure: the token-AST ratchet is not a compiler/name-resolution or deployed-binary identity proof, and it does not prove the typed plan's row set, arbitrary indirect post-helper row mutation, sled implementation semantics, or operator/service reachability. The immutable plan/raw-apply/readback contract and rollback, cardinality, and reopen tests supply that remaining row and runtime evidence.
- Current formal-core metadata now includes recursive_block_v2 verifier-surface binding, packed SmallWood active spend-auth binding, SmallWood Goldilocks auth-link vector conformance, wallet DA ciphertext parser admission, the stablecoin mint exception payload surface, wallet stablecoin policy payload construction, native stablecoin manifest authorization with lifecycle-open rejection, bounded PQ SecureSession frame-vector conformance, PQ transport-completion and network/native wrapper-completion vector conformance, production PQ channel seed-source separation, public metadata privacy certificates, network/PQ frame-resource admission conformance, commitment-tree/nullifier integrity certificates, materialized ledger-integrity end-to-end review, generic bounded-request Rust conformance, native sync bounded-request response-count admission, schema-v2 abstract bridge mint/replay policy Rust conformance, bridge final canonical replay publication facts, bridge release-disabled mint/replay hardening, accepted-mode release posture certificate coverage, pre-heavy DoS certificate coverage, and schema-v2 fork-choice selected-tip/native-wrapper conformance in addition to the privacy open-assumption boundary plus materialized transfer authorized-delta, public stablecoin-exception, and output-slot artifact projections: the claims ledger contains 121 theorem-backed claims plus one approved tombstone, with 2745 named Lean theorem declarations, 107 production-eligible claims, and 65 explicit residual risks; the blueprint contains 121 nodes, 519 dependency edges, 677 falsification cases, 230 implementation bindings, 145 order constraints, 174 result obligations, and 123 dominance constraints.
- `formal/lean` is the pinned Lean 4 project for machine-checked Hegemon kernels. The detailed bullets below name the proved predicates and generated conformance vectors; passing `formal-core` is release-gate evidence for those narrow executable rules, not a proof of hash security, proof-system soundness, privacy, network finality, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The consensus PoW admission kernel proves checked u64 next-height arithmetic, compact-target rejection, strict timestamp policy, hash-threshold rejection, and fixed-width Work48 cumulative-work behavior with `Hegemon.Consensus.checkedNextU64_rejects_max`, `Hegemon.Consensus.checkedNextU64_accepts_predecessor`, `Hegemon.Consensus.powAdmission_rejects_height_overflow`, and the companion compact-target, timestamp, work-addition, and valid-admission theorems, including `Hegemon.Consensus.compactTarget_rejects_shifted_zero_target` for nonzero-mantissa compact bits that decode to target zero. The same Lean module now pins the executable retarget target-adjustment clamp with `Hegemon.Consensus.retargetConstants_match_consensus`, `Hegemon.Consensus.adjustedTimespan_clamps_fast_blocks`, `Hegemon.Consensus.adjustedTimespan_clamps_slow_blocks`, and the companion `retargetTarget_*` theorems; compact retarget re-encoding with `Hegemon.Consensus.targetToCompact_easy_roundtrip`, `Hegemon.Consensus.targetToCompact_roundtrip_easy_target`, `Hegemon.Consensus.retargetBits_expected_timespan_keeps_bits`, and `Hegemon.Consensus.retargetBits_rejects_invalid_previous_bits`; and retarget-boundary scheduling/history with `Hegemon.Consensus.retargetAnchorSteps_boundary_requires_window`, `Hegemon.Consensus.expectedPowBitsSchedule_missing_history_rejects`, `Hegemon.Consensus.expectedPowBitsSchedule_expected_timespan_keeps_bits`, and the companion scheduler theorems. `gen_pow_vectors` emits examples that Rust `evaluate_pow_admission`, `consensus::reward::adjusted_timespan`, `consensus::reward::retarget_target`, `target_to_compact`, `pow_retarget_anchor_steps`, `evaluate_pow_bits_schedule`, and the light-client Work48 helpers must match. Native node regressions now exercise the same schedule through app-path work templates and announced-block admission: the first genesis-anchored boundary inherits launch bits, the next fast retarget window lowers the target, a stale fixed-difficulty child rejects before import, and a following slow retarget window eases difficulty only through the bounded adjustment factor. Focused regressions reject u64 height overflow at both generic consensus admission and `consensus-light-client::verify_pow_header`, accept retargeted children only through the explicit expected-bits light-client verifier, and reject generic consensus cumulative work that would exceed the 48-byte work domain. This does not prove SHA-256 implementation equivalence, timestamp honesty, retarget economics, network orphan policy, long-range finality, or complete light-client soundness.
- The consensus proof-policy kernel proves the active independent-proof shape with `Hegemon.Consensus.empty_clean_accepts`, `nonempty_requires_tx_artifacts`, `independent_rejects_proven_batch`, `independent_rejects_block_artifact`, and `independent_complete_accepts`; retained recursive theorem rows cover historical compatibility. `gen_proof_policy_vectors` checks production `evaluate_block_proof_policy` before native leaf verification, claim use, historical aggregate verification, or tree transition.
- The native tx-leaf admission kernel proves the cheap consensus envelope/profile/size/hash table before mandatory native backend verification with `Hegemon.Consensus.NativeTxLeafAdmission.accepts_iff_admission_preconditions`, `Hegemon.Consensus.NativeTxLeafAdmission.valid_admission_requires_backend_verification`, and companion rejection theorems. The model has no cache input or cache-hit result: every accepted structural admission returns `needs_backend_verification`. `gen_native_tx_leaf_admission_vectors` emits examples that Rust `evaluate_native_tx_leaf_admission` must match, and the focused diagnostic-cache regression ensures a populated cache never bypasses exact backend verification. The blueprint requires result propagation and dominance in `verify_native_tx_leaf_artifact_record` before backend verification, statement binding, or decode, and also requires `NativeTxLeafVerifier::verify_tx_artifact` to tail-propagate `verify_native_tx_leaf_artifact_record(...).map(...)` at the product entry point. Skipped edge: `NativeTxLeafVerifier::verify_block_artifact` is not an implemented method on this verifier.
- The receipt-root admission kernel proves the cheap consensus payload/envelope/size/count/statement/metadata tables around native receipt-root verification with `Hegemon.Consensus.ReceiptRootAdmission.payload_accepts_iff_preconditions`, `Hegemon.Consensus.ReceiptRootAdmission.artifact_accepts_iff_preconditions`, `Hegemon.Consensus.ReceiptRootAdmission.statement_accepts_iff_preconditions`, and `Hegemon.Consensus.ReceiptRootAdmission.verified_metadata_accepts_iff_preconditions`, plus companion rejection theorems. `gen_receipt_root_admission_vectors` emits examples that the production receipt-root admission helpers in `consensus/src/proof.rs` must match. The blueprint binds payload admission in `ParallelProofVerifier::verify_block_with_backend` before `verify_block_artifact`, and binds artifact, statement, and metadata admission in `ReceiptRootVerifier::verify_block_artifact` with propagated results and dominance before native tx-leaf record verification and backend receipt-root verification.
- The native sidecar upload admission kernel proves DA staging request-count caps, staged sidecar capacity with replacement semantics, proof binding-hash metadata admission, decoded proof byte caps, and decoded proof binding-hash equality with the upload key with `Hegemon.Native.SidecarUploadAdmission` theorems, including `Hegemon.Native.SidecarUploadAdmission.proof_binding_hash_mismatch_rejects`. `gen_sidecar_upload_admission_vectors` emits examples, including `proof_binding_hash_mismatch`, that Rust sidecar upload helpers used by `da_submitCiphertexts` and `da_submitProofs` must match. `Hegemon.Native.SidecarUploadRawJsonProjection` adds representative raw request-byte vectors for strict ciphertext/proof upload JSON decoding, missing/non-array required fields, unknown fields, byte parser failures, too-many arrays, and proof metadata/empty-proof rejection; `gen_sidecar_upload_raw_json_projection_vectors` drives those bytes through production serde_json and the live DA upload request decoders. This does not prove RPC authentication, unsafe RPC exposure safety, complete serde_json/base64/hex parser correctness, ciphertext hash security or implementation equivalence, sled durability, DA sidecar availability, native tx-leaf proof soundness, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The pre-heavy-work resource-bound surface is a Lean composition over accepted `ResourceBudgetAdmission`, `RpcAdmission`, `SidecarUploadAdmission`, `MineableActionAdmission`, `ActionRequestProjectionAdmission`, `TransferActionPayloadAdmission`, `CandidateArtifactAdmission`, bridge resource admission, tx-leaf artifact parser/resource inputs, receipt-root schedule/resource inputs, and native sync admission. `Hegemon.Native.PreHeavyWorkResourceBoundSurface.accepted_preheavy_resource_bound_surface_exposes_bounds` packages pending-action and staged-proof byte caps, timestamp row caps, hex/base64 byte caps, JSON-RPC batch caps, sidecar request/capacity/proof preconditions, and mineable-action preconditions into one review surface before expensive proof or block-template work. `action_request_projection_accepts_implies_preheavy_bounds` derives strict submit-action JSON, empty kernel-envelope, supported route, nullifier, public-args, and exact route-payload predicates from accepted action-request projection before heavy work. `accepted_preheavy_public_input_parser_admission_bounds_verification_paths` adds those action-request facts, transfer proof/ciphertext caps, recursive candidate tx/proof caps, bridge receipt/message caps, tx-leaf byte-shape plus raw/decoded/item/proof/aggregate/work request facts, and receipt-root nonzero expected-leaf, exact leaf/fold schedule, and raw/decoded/item/max-item/aggregate/work request facts before heavy verification or receipt-root folding, while `sync_path_accepts_implies_preheavy_bounds` adds bounded sync decode, range, response-count, and resource-count facts before sync import work. `accepted_preheavy_work_dos_bound_certificate` packages the full pre-heavy surface into one release-review certificate, and `accepted_preheavy_dos_certificate_covers_all_public_input_cost_classes` exposes the 22 production public-input cost families covered by that certificate, with parser correctness and benchmark caps carried as explicit assumptions. `Hegemon.Resource.BoundedRequestAdmission` is now both the reusable formal table and a production-conformance target: it proves raw-byte, decoded-byte, item-count, per-item, aggregate-byte, and work-unit caps with exact-limit acceptance and deterministic first-failing-cap rejection, `gen_bounded_request_admission_vectors` is checked against production `evaluate_native_bounded_request_admission`, native block-action decode binds it through the pre-decode byte budget, native tx-leaf and receipt-root vectors bind parser-derived resource projections to decoded `superneo-hegemon` artifacts, and native sync response-count admission binds it through a zero-allocation item-count gate before import work. It does not prove JSON parser correctness, receipt-root cryptographic fold soundness, RPC authentication, unsafe-method exposure safety, every remaining parser-family binding, DA availability, sled durability, proof-system soundness, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The network frame-resource admission kernel proves the reusable cheap codec gate for Hegemon network wire and PQ Noise postcard frames. `Hegemon.Network.FrameResourceAdmission.evaluate_frame_decode_accepts_iff` proves accepted frame decoding is exactly byte-cap, marker-match, postcard-decode-success, and no-trailing-byte admission; companion rejection theorems pin oversize-before-marker/parser precedence, missing-marker rejection, postcard failure rejection, and trailing-byte rejection. `evaluate_frame_encode_accepts_iff` proves outbound marker-plus-body length admission, `frame_kind_magic_length_four` pins the HNW/PNH/PNS/PNT marker width, and `pq_session_plaintext_plus_tag_eq_ciphertext_bound` connects PQ session plaintext admission to the encrypted frame bound. `gen_frame_resource_admission_vectors` emits constants plus accept/reject cases that Rust checks against `network::wire` and `pq-noise::codec`. The companion queue-resource kernel proves peer-send and event-channel byte-budget reserve/send accounting: `queue_reserve_accepts_iff`, `accepted_queue_reserve_exposes_facts`, and `accepted_bounded_send_exposes_facts` pin message cap, usize overflow, aggregate queued-byte cap, accepted-send increment, and full/closed rollback behavior, while `bounded_send_reserve_rejection_precedes_channel_state` pins rejection precedence before channel-state effects. The peer-store capacity kernel proves retained entries are bounded by `max_entries`, match the recency-prefix subset, and partition with the dropped recency suffix; `gen_peer_store_capacity_admission_vectors` is checked against production `PeerStore::enforce_max_entries` for retained IDs, dropped IDs, prefix status, and dropped overflow absence. `gen_queue_resource_admission_vectors` is checked against production `network_backend` queue helpers by the formal-core gate. Focused runtime gates additionally check handshake-frame cap staging before secure-wire promotion, handshake timeout/backlog limits, dialable/capped address retention, and nonblocking peer send loops. This does not prove postcard implementation correctness, primitive cryptographic security, tokio length-delimited transport correctness, scheduler fairness, peer honesty, network liveness, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The commitment-tree content refinement kernel proves ordered row-content equivalence for accepted raw-ingress publication. `Hegemon.Native.CommitmentTreeContentRefinement.canonical_commitment_rows_bind_ordered_decoded_content`, `canonical_commitment_rows_bind_ordered_decoded_indexes`, and `accepted_raw_ingress_full_byte_publication_binds_commitment_tree_content` package accepted raw-ingress full-byte publication, accepted ledger/tree replay, commitment-root publication, replayed leaf-cursor facts, and canonical commitment rows over one raw witness. The same module now packages replay-set and ciphertext content: `canonical_nullifier_rows_bind_ordered_decoded_content`, `canonical_bridge_replay_rows_bind_planned_replay_keys`, and `accepted_raw_ingress_full_byte_publication_binds_replay_set_content` prove canonical nullifier rows and bridge replay rows expose the exact ordered decoded/planned replay-set content, while `native_publication_rows_equivalence_binds_ciphertext_content` and `accepted_native_publication_path_family_binds_ciphertext_content` prove exact ciphertext index/archive rows across raw ingress, mined commit, announced import, sync import, reorg replay, canonical-index rebuild, `block_range`, and startup repair. `accepted_native_publication_path_family_binds_commitment_replay_content` lifts exact ordered commitment/nullifier/bridge replay content through the same path family. The production consensus tree now also exposes opt-in `CommitmentTreeState::append_with_certificate`, which shares the hot append mutation body and records a replayable append trace with prior/result root-history tails, leaf index/value, sibling orientation/default choices, per-level parents, and result root/count; formal-core gates this with `cargo test -p consensus append_` plus Lean-generated `Hegemon.Consensus.CommitmentTreeAppend` mutation vectors checked against the production certificate and real Merkle replay. That same gate now checks oversized consensus batch extension rejects before root, leaf-count, frontier, or history mutation, while the state-merkle oracle checks the corresponding production tree batch rejection. `decodedRowsFollowLeafCursor_of_action_plan_accepts`, `orderedDecodedCommitmentIndexes_eq_rangePrime_of_cursor`, and `rawIngressAppendSummaries_indexes_eq_orderedDecodedCommitmentIndexes_of_action_plan` now prove that accepted row-start plans induce exact flattened append indexes. `raw_ingress_commitment_tree_content_binds_append_publication_surface` and `accepted_raw_ingress_full_byte_publication_binds_commitment_append_publication` attach that append-summary/index/count surface to accepted raw-ingress commitment-root and leaf-cursor publication facts, exposing equality between append-summary leaf indexes, ordered decoded commitment indexes, and canonical commitment-row indexes. This moves the target past root-cursor and row-count equality into exact ordered canonical publication rows, exact ciphertext row content, a production mutation certificate hook, Lean-vector-checked mutation control flow, atomic batch-extension rejection, and accepted append-publication packaging through the executable row-start model, but it still does not prove arbitrary parser internals, Merkle/replay-key/nullifier/ciphertext hash security or equivalence, storage durability below accepted predicates, positive inbound bridge publication while the release verifier is disabled, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The native staged-ciphertext reload kernel proves the startup per-entry admission/drop table with `Hegemon.Native.StagedCiphertextReload.accepts_iff_staged_ciphertext_reload_preconditions` and companion rejection-precedence theorems. `gen_staged_ciphertext_reload_vectors` emits examples that Rust `evaluate_native_staged_ciphertext_reload` must match before `load_staged_sizes_with_limits` restores proposer-local sidecar size metadata. This does not prove sled atomicity, filesystem durability, I/O error behavior, BLAKE3 ciphertext-hash security or implementation equivalence, DA sidecar availability, native tx-leaf proof soundness, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The native staged-proof reload kernel proves the startup per-entry admission/drop table with `Hegemon.Native.StagedProofReload.accepts_iff_staged_proof_reload_preconditions` and companion rejection-precedence theorems. `gen_staged_proof_reload_vectors` emits examples that Rust `evaluate_native_staged_proof_reload` must match before `load_staged_proofs_with_limits` restores proposer-local proof sidecars. This does not prove sled atomicity, filesystem durability, I/O error behavior, binding-hash security or implementation equivalence, proof byte parsing, DA sidecar availability, native tx-leaf proof soundness, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The native sync admission kernel proves response-range, missing-request, zero-cap, saturating-edge, and inbound response-count behavior with `Hegemon.Native.SyncAdmission` theorems. `gen_sync_admission_vectors` emits examples that Rust sync helpers used by response serving, catch-up requests, and inbound response rejection must match. Runtime response serving now measures the encrypted wire frame and soft-chunks sync responses below the transport maximum so block catch-up does not monopolize proof-frame queues used by live pending-action relay, and dead outbound sync requests expire quickly so a dropped response cannot stall catch-up for minutes. Runtime mining startup caps effective worker count against available CPU parallelism, reserves service headroom, and applies a default background-mining worker cap so P2P, RPC, and sync-response work can run while a node mines. Runtime pending-action rebroadcast is likewise bounded by action count and serialized mempool bytes. `Hegemon.Native.SyncRawIngress` adds Lean-owned raw sync request/empty-response bytes plus malformed-byte examples checked against production `decode_sync_message`, `encode_sync_message`, `native_sync_response_range`, `admit_and_sort_native_sync_response_blocks`, and `NativeSyncResponseImportProgress`. `Hegemon.Native.SyncResponseImport` adds the imported-response control-flow gate: count admission precedes sort/import, sorted heights are deterministic, import progress stops on the first error, imported and attempted counts stay bounded by the response, and follow-up requests require a nonempty response plus a still-ahead peer. `gen_sync_response_import_vectors` is checked against production `admit_and_sort_native_sync_response_blocks` and `NativeSyncResponseImportProgress`. Production bindings additionally require non-genesis canonical rows served over sync to decode native action bodies and verify the decoded action root before response publication. `Hegemon.Native.SyncBlockReplayPublication.accepted_sync_block_response_binds_raw_canonical_publication` composes accepted sync decode/count admission with exact block-action decode and raw canonical publication so synced raw block batches carry replayed supply, leaf-cursor, commitment-root, nullifier, and bridge replay-key facts; `accepted_sync_block_response_with_resource_bounds_binds_raw_canonical_publication_from_decoded_rows` adds the accepted bounded-request item-count package and proves it matches the response count and raw block batch length. This does not prove SCALE codec correctness, arbitrary postcard or sync/block parser internals, PQ Noise transport security, full PoW/header validity beyond the metadata projection helper, fork-choice convergence, peer honesty, network liveness, storage durability, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The native mineable-action admission kernel proves the active block-template gate with `Hegemon.Native.MineableActionAdmission.accepts_iff_mineable_preconditions`: every candidate artifact rejects as `retired_candidate_artifact`, plain actions remain eligible, and sidecar transfers require matching locally staged ciphertext metadata and size. `gen_mineable_action_admission_vectors` checks the production selector against those decisions. This does not prove ciphertext hash security, DA durability, transaction-proof soundness, global miner fairness, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The native mined-work admission kernel proves the local mined-block freshness gate with `Hegemon.Native.MinedWorkAdmission.accepts_iff_mined_work_preconditions`, covering current-best parent-hash agreement, checked next-height arithmetic, u64 height overflow rejection, and rejection precedence. `Hegemon.Native.MinedBlockCommitPublication.accepted_mined_block_commit_publication_facts` composes accepted mined-work freshness, native block-commitment admission, and atomic mined-block commit-manifest admission into one local publication surface exposing parent/height preconditions, header/body/supply commitment preconditions, mined-block manifest kind, and atomic manifest preconditions. `gen_mined_work_admission_vectors` emits examples that Rust `evaluate_native_mined_work_admission` must match before pending-action replay, body commitment admission, supply accounting, and PoW metadata verification; `gen_mined_block_commit_publication_vectors` now checks the composed production helper order for mined publication itself: mined-work rejection, block-commitment rejection, mined-manifest kind mismatch, then manifest-shape rejection. The blueprint also binds `verify_native_pow_meta` inside `import_mined_block` and requires it to occur before `commit_mined_block_atomically`, `publish_mined_state`, and `broadcast_block_announce`; `commit_mined_block_atomically` must occur before persisted-head readback, state publication, and broadcast. `mined_invalid_pow_does_not_mutate_pending_state` keeps invalid local seals from partially mutating pending, commitment, or supply state, and `action_block_commit_reloads_canonical_sled_state` checks that mined and reorg action blocks reload the best header, height index, block record, commitment state, archived ciphertext, and pending-action tree from sled after reopening. Mined import now builds the next in-memory state on a clone, commits the block record, height index, best pointer, canonical state/replay/ciphertext indexes, and pending-action removals in one sled transaction, flushes the durability barrier, reads back the persisted head rows, and only then publishes that state to the running node. Nonwinning announced side-branch block-record reload is covered separately by the block replay-refinement implementation-binding gate. This does not prove work-template construction, action validity, body replay, supply accounting, PoW hash-threshold validity beyond the production verifier call, sled transaction implementation correctness, filesystem/fsync durability, fork-choice liveness, or complete native-node equivalence.

Arrival-time-insensitive semantic hashes key single-flight and the bounded ten-minute negative cache; only deterministic proof/decode failures enter that cache. Peer, local, and mining-template proof work each have a one-lane class cap under a three-lane global cap. Mining snapshots and revalidates the tip/action set, isolates batch failures in parallel, atomically quarantines only independently invalid actions, and retries survivors once; startup sanitizes persisted rows through the same proof boundary. Externally submitted candidate and coinbase actions reject before mempool budgeting. These controls move the required proof verification to admission and reuse the production tx-leaf verification cache; they do not weaken block-import re-verification.

The canonical sync tip-extension path replays each block on carried candidate state before evaluating one `TipExtensionBatchCommit` manifest, executing one transaction, and flushing before state publication. Block artifact verification and canonical-index rebuild now consume those same materialized ciphertext bytes before tx-leaf artifact construction or canonical row derivation, so they cannot use a weaker sidecar fetch path. The blueprint implementation-binding gate rejects the claim if any non-test caller stops referencing the helper or if replay/memory planning stops using `materialize_native_action_payloads` and `plan_materialized_action_effects`. The same blueprint gate enforces declared order constraints requiring the helper to precede payload/artifact validation, the atomic mined-block sled commit, state publication, and broadcast in mined import; deeper payload/artifact validation plus nonwinning side-branch block-record persistence and winning explicit-chain reorganization in announced import; the sync batch mutation/publication boundaries; and memory mutation in canonical replay. Canonical reorg state itself is committed through `commit_reorg_state_atomically` before `publish_reorganized_state`, and `reorg_action_block_commit_reloads_canonical_sled_state` checks best/header/block-record/index/state/archive/pending reload after reopening. Startup canonical-index repair is separately bound to `commit_canonical_index_repair_atomically` and covered by `startup_canonical_index_repair_rebuilds_archive_atomically`. Nonwinning announced side-branch persistence is bound to `persist_noncanonical_block_record`, and `nonwinning_announced_side_branch_record_reloads_without_canonicalizing` checks hash-addressed reload without canonical height replacement. Canonical replay now also re-runs `verify_native_block_artifacts_locked` before memory application, and `reorg_replay_rechecks_historical_side_branch_artifacts_before_publish` checks that a durable side branch with invalid historical artifacts cannot publish a reorg. The formal-core wrapper also runs `announced_block_replay_commitment_mismatch_precedes_payload_validation` and `materialized_sidecar_ciphertext` regressions. This does not prove parser-internal arbitrary raw SCALE/bincode decoding, full raw-action or raw block-replay equivalence, Merkle tree content/hash implementation equivalence, hash-key uniqueness, tx/recursive proof soundness, sled batch/transaction implementation correctness, operating-system fsync semantics below the accepted durability predicate, deep-fork convergence, or complete native-node equivalence.
- The production `action_byte_drift` conformance gate now rejects exact-decodable substituted action bodies on announced import before persistence/publication, startup replay before accepting nonempty corrupted state, reorg replay before publishing a winning child over a corrupted side parent, and sync `block_range` before serving a canonical block body whose valid bytes no longer match the committed action root. This strengthens implementation-equivalence evidence without adding normal-path replay work; parser internals and the complete native-node equivalence theorem remain open.
- `Hegemon.Native.PendingActionByteParserRefinement.accepted_pending_action_byte_parser_refines_wire_replay_rows` now splits the parser/wire row-count part out of that byte-admission layer: accepted block-action decode binds declared transaction count to actual action payload count, accepted wire replay binds projected action rows to accepted decoded action count, and the publication theorem derives projected-row agreement from those facts plus the production declared-count binding instead of taking row agreement as an assumption. `Hegemon.Native.PendingActionByteReplayRowCountBinding.pending_action_field_projection_binds_decoded_production_fields` and `accepted_pending_action_raw_bytes_bind_production_field_projection_rows` then package the accepted exact-decode/projection-evidence production field projection rows for decoded, validation, materialized, planned, wire, and canonical surfaces, while `pending_action_raw_byte_projection_requires_canonical_decode` derives accepted exact pending-action and block-action decode from that package. Raw byte coverage is supplied by production Rust gates, including rejection of trailing action bytes before canonical-index row projection. Parser-internal arbitrary raw SCALE/bincode correctness remains a separate target.
- The native bridge action payload admission kernel proves decoded bridge-family routing, clean state-delta scope, supported bridge action IDs, outbound payload presence, inbound proof-receipt presence, inbound replay-key/message binding, Hegemon destination binding, inbound payload-hash binding, and rejection precedence with `Hegemon.Native.BridgeActionPayloadAdmission.accepts_iff_payload_preconditions` and companion theorems. `gen_bridge_action_payload_admission_vectors` emits examples that Rust `evaluate_native_bridge_action_payload_admission` must match before bridge receipt verification.
- The native RISC Zero release-verifier kernel proves the fail-closed release decision table after bridge action prechecks with `Hegemon.Native.Risc0ReleaseVerifier.accepts_iff_release_preconditions`, `Hegemon.Native.Risc0ReleaseVerifier.release_build_never_accepts`, `Hegemon.Native.Risc0ReleaseVerifier.release_disabled_rejects`, and companion image-id/journal rejection-precedence theorems. `gen_risc0_release_verifier_vectors` emits examples that Rust `evaluate_native_risc0_release_verifier` must match, the inbound bridge regression still verifies that replay-key, destination, and payload-hash tampering reject before the disabled verifier path, and the blueprint now requires `verify_inbound_bridge_receipt` to propagate `verify_risc0_bridge_receipt`. `Hegemon.Native.RawIngressBridgePendingActionPublication.release_disabled_blocks_receipt_mint_replay_policy_before_replay`, `release_disabled_precludes_accepted_inbound_bridge_mint_policy`, and `release_disabled_precludes_raw_ingress_bridge_mint_replay_policy_publication` connect that fail-closed release posture to bridge publication: a policy receipt bit sourced from a disabled release verifier rejects before replay/mint and cannot produce raw-ingress bridge mint-replay publication facts. The evaluator's explicit `expect_err` rejection sentinels are not yet expressible as result obligations in the current binding grammar. This does not enable RISC Zero receipt acceptance; it proves the current PQ-only release node fails closed until a PQ-clean verifier replaces it and blocks receipt-mint policy publication while disabled.
- The consensus header-preimage kernel proves the signing preimage domain and fixed-field byte layout, signing-preimage independence from signature/BFT bitmap/PoW seal payloads, BFT bitmap tag/length encoding, PoW seal tag/nonce/pow_bits encoding, and absent-auth tags with `Hegemon.Consensus.Header.signingPreimage_independent_of_auth_payloads` and the companion length/tag theorems. `gen_header_vectors` emits exact byte preimages that Rust `BlockHeader::signing_preimage_v1` and `BlockHeader::full_header_preimage_v1` must match. This does not prove ML-DSA unforgeability, ML-DSA implementation equivalence, key-registration governance, or miner key custody. Header signing preimage byte grammar is covered separately by the consensus header-preimage kernel.
- The former native miner-identity kernel and generator describe a retired identity-bearing metadata experiment and are not imported, generated, or credited by the active formal core. Identity-free codec vectors plus focused Rust regressions require exact active round trips and explicit rejection of unsigned/signed V1 and interim identity-bearing V2 bytes before replay or mutation. These checks establish era separation and field absence; they do not prove proof-of-work security, hash security, storage durability, or complete native-node equivalence.
