- The SmallWood no-counterfeit formalization is split at the actual implementation boundaries and is conditional, not deployed authority. `SmallWoodProductionAcceptanceClosure` defines `CallerSuppliedVerifierEvidence` for modeled parser, SHA-512 counter-mode transcript, rejection-sampled challenge, reconstructed PCS/PIOP/DECS message, and authenticated-row facts; no theorem constructs that record from every accepted compiled Rust execution. `SmallWoodCmsQrom` bounds adaptive acceptance without a valid extraction only in its ideal logical-oracle model. `SmallWoodProductionSupplyChain` reaches block supply after a caller supplies per-proof extraction success and canonical semantic refinement, plus separate Poseidon2 constraint-digest refinement and pair-local no-collision assumptions. The generated map pins the contiguous 78-field public statement, both complete sparse linear tables, 11,604 nonlinear expression nodes, 1,722 ordered roots, eight disjoint family spans, geometry, and the program digest. The exact-map equation record is not yet proved to refine the legacy canonical `AcceptedTransactionRelation`; deployed SHA-512/QROM instantiation and arbitrary compiler/native-machine refinement also remain open.
- The retained V4 proof-wire hardening is executable on both sides of the Rust/Lean boundary. Rust rejects non-canonical field words and malformed compact shapes, exact-consumes and canonically re-encodes accepted legacy proofs, and checks Lean-generated wire vectors. Its rejection sampling, first-valid-nonce rule, and exact no-grinding arithmetic are reusable controls, but their dimensions and wrapper do not establish V6 security or authorization. `ReceiptRoot` remains absent from any production-validity registry.
- The isolated `formal/crypto` package mechanizes CCS and quadratic-program semantics, canonical proof and transaction bytes, exact SHA-512 counter-mode expansion and rejection sampling, finite quantum-oracle state, masking, compact-Merkle extraction, round-by-round interactive extraction, and an ideal logical-oracle adaptive CMS/QROM bound. Its supply theorem is a separate conditional theorem over caller-supplied extraction, semantic-refinement, and Poseidon2 premises. `scripts/check_formal_crypto.sh` builds the top-level package and audits the exported declarations against the kernel axiom allowlist; the package's indexed security-authority type has no deployed-end-to-end constructor. The remaining authorization boundary is explicit: no theorem transfers the ideal bound to deployed SHA-512, constructs the evidence record from arbitrary compiled Rust/machine executions, or proves the exact V4 row model refines the legacy canonical transaction semantics. Tag release therefore runs `scripts/check_smallwood_production_authorization.py config/formal-security-claims.json` before package verification or binary builds. The checker accepts only if `formal.deployed-smallwood-no-counterfeit-critical-path` is uniquely present, production-eligible, has every required and future authority boolean true, and records at least 128 concrete post-quantum bits. Fixture tests exercise authorized and fail-closed documents, while the workflow policy checker rejects removal, conditional execution, shell soft-fail, or `continue-on-error`; ordinary PR CI tests the checker and wiring without executing the intentionally failing live authorization decision.
- The local Poseidon2 degree-annihilation review is checked in as `docs/crypto/poseidon2_degree_annihilation_cryptanalysis.md` with a reproducible JSON report generated by `scripts/analyze_poseidon2_degree_annihilation.py --check`. That pass analyzes ePrint 2026/1254 against the exact Hegemon Goldilocks width-12, rate/capacity 6/6, `x^7`, 8-full-round, 22-partial-round Poseidon2 tuple and finds no practical transfer of the reduced-round CICO-2 attacks to Hegemon's six-limb digest surface. Its exact integer output-space calculation records a 127-bit generic quantum-collision exponent ceiling for six Goldilocks words, so the tool explicitly does not authorize a strict 128-bit primitive claim. External cryptanalysis must still cover degree-annihilation, skipping-class, CICO-k, collision, preimage, sponge-mode, and SmallWood transcript adaptations.
- The fresh width-16 Poseidon2 candidate is implemented additively in `circuits/transaction-core/src/poseidon2_width16.rs` and pinned by `config/poseidon2-width16-v1.json` as `hegemon-p2w16-v1-114a4e7eb2684d29`. `scripts/check_poseidon2_width16_parameters.py --check` independently regenerates the 150 Grain constants, re-derives the base `RF=6/RP=20` inequality optimum and the selected `RF=8/RP=22` margins, checks the `M4 ⊗ P4` and `D+J` matrices, binds canonical digests and KATs, and rejects any production-authorization flip. The standalone exact-version p3 reference under `tools/poseidon2-width16-p3-reference/` checks the Hegemon permutation, fixed `14 -> 7` compression, and sponge lengths 0/7/8/9/16/34/120 while retaining stock p3-goldilocks 0.6.3 as a required negative control because its matrix/diagonal instance differs. Seven outputs provide approximately 149.33 bits of generic BHT collision work, but that arithmetic and the local 2026/306, 2026/1254, and 2026/1760 screens are not production security proofs. External exact-tuple cryptanalysis, protocol-wide QROM accounting, native/relation refinement, consensus versioning, and cross-language vectors remain mandatory; the manifest is intentionally `production_authorized=false`.
- The historical binary-backend tournament preserved self-contained transaction authority before optimizing bytes. At pinned Binius64 commit `3f96163049f680b2909f6545690bd929f1b48c44`, honest raw transcripts measured 298,304/309,664/321,120 bytes for the wrapped-ZK path and 246,048/327,984/414,752 bytes for direct IronSpartan at 1/8/64 BLAKE2b compressions, all under an insufficient 96-bit query-only/SHA-256 profile. The old 520-action budget permitted only 124,080 artifact bytes, so those implementations did not qualify. Other M4, BaseFold, and Flock measurements likewise used incomplete relations, non-ZK profiles, or sub-PQ128 security. That screen temporarily selected one standalone binary-native SHAKE256 proof with a 1 MiB parser cap and 512 KiB target, but later relation, zero-knowledge, and security evidence disqualified it. Its later inactive V6 SmallWood-plus-SHAKE leader was also disqualified; no binary-backend result qualified. The selected current candidate instead retains the compact SmallWood engine with the Poseidon2 HGV8RP03 relation. Aggregation or sidecars may optimize local work but cannot replace the unchanged proof carried through admission, relay, blocks, sync, reorg, and fresh replay. Release still requires a checked composed QROM calculation above 128 bits.
- The historical standalone prototype made that temporary selection executable without changing production consensus. Its `HEG-S4V2` one-input/two-output Pay1x2 core, 478-byte adapter, 40-permutation schedule, proof-size runs, mutation cases, and action/network boundary tests are retained as narrow prototype evidence. They do not cover the complete 2-input/2-output, all-mask, stablecoin, and authorization surface; upstream also uses a 96-bit query budget, SHA-256, and GF(2^128). Its modeled 129.415-bit screen is not a composed deployed-QROM certificate. None of its measured bytes is a V6 frontier point, and every implementation/review capability remains false.
- The local proof autoresearch method keeps the digest-sealed semantic evaluator outside candidate control and applies one allowlisted Binius source patch at a time to a disposable post-baseline tree. It validates every patch header and independently enumerates the actual post-apply tree, accepting only modifications to existing regular `crates/*/src/*.rs` files. The trusted patch-preparation phase executes no candidate code; compilation sees the prepared source as read-only under a default-deny-read profile limited to the owned evaluator, offline Cargo/Rust closure, and required operating-system files. Candidate-linked tests/provers/verifiers then run as sibling sandbox processes under a second default-deny-read profile exposing only their exact executable, target/tmp, and operating-system runtime closure while denying forks, non-self execs, launchd jobs, Mach/XPC lookup, POSIX IPC, external signals, process-group changes, and network access. A smoke gate verifies both phases cannot read an outside secret. A result enters its tamper-evident ledger only after the full composed Pay1x2 mutation and fresh-forgery suite passes on an actual canonical proof; size models may reject work cheaply but can never promote it. Strict-PQ128 and upstream weak-profile results have separate Pareto frontiers. Prototype measurements remain candidate-linked research evidence, while strict qualification additionally requires an owned exact proof artifact and a sealed independent HGSP/action/statement verifier; neither a stored ledger flag nor the arithmetic budget calculator can authorize a strict result. Runs are globally serialized and admitted only with at least 28 GiB free on the shared APFS volume; the controller terminates the child process group below a non-lowerable 20 GiB reserve, above an 8 GiB process-group RSS cap, a sampled 4 GiB Cargo-target or 5 GiB run-root cap, or on bounded-output/time failure, then deletes only its exact UUID-marked directory. The historical sealed ledger retains the 350,800-byte all-private and 309,072-byte Luna proof points. A versioned evaluator policy that additionally admits only `crates/iop-prover/` measured the terminal-message patch on the same 478-byte statement, 40-permutation, 1,883,192-row relation: **272,176 proof bytes and 272,188 envelope bytes**, 36,896 bytes (11.94%) below Luna. Every frozen verification fact passed, including fresh forged-public negative controls, and the retained one-entry trial chain verifies at head `1735398a62f5fe058d8909ff263201eba49655f5d3e87a22234269c16594a720`. All measured points remain nonqualifying 96-bit/SHA-256/GF(2^128) research evidence. Strict completion requires two matching clean live reproductions and remains false until the independent backend, zero-knowledge, QROM, formal-refinement, and serialization capabilities are real.
- The current proof-wire pass composes two further injective encodings with that terminal-message profile. Canonical compact Merkle multiproofs sort and deduplicate the Fiat-Shamir query indices, send each distinct leaf once, and serialize only sibling frontier nodes whose subtrees contain no query; the verifier derives the entire schedule without prover metadata, authenticates the same root, and then restores the original query order. FRI verifier-known-value elision removes each selected scalar already supplied by the preceding authenticated fold claim; the verifier inserts that claim at the derived offset, reconstructs and hashes the complete leaf, repeats the selected-value equality, and performs the unchanged fold. A focused duplicate-query/multi-offset test passes. The full supervised relation measured **244,240 proof bytes and a 244,252-byte envelope**, with every semantic, composition, action, envelope, mutation, exact-consumption, range/carry, and fresh-forgery gate true. The retained entry digest is `51c94235a189fcbf826f1fb387ccfb5bf78876d41bb90839619971346d0fb6ed`. This is standalone weak-profile wire evidence only: symbolic recursive parsing is unchanged, complete zero knowledge and strict PQ128 remain unproved, and the strict frontier is empty.
- The native M4 pass expresses the specialized Pay1x2 core as one main circuit and uses no numbered chips, whose call interface is not yet constrained. It binds that experiment's 2,448-byte private witness and 478 public HGS2 bytes, inlines all 40 SHAKE256 Keccak-f permutations, and compiles to 24,160 native AND constraints with a `2^15` committed tier. The stock rate sweep measured 129,424/109,264/110,864/119,728 proof bytes at inverse-rate logs one through four. Applying the already verified terminal-message, compact-Merkle-frontier, and verifier-known-FRI-value encodings changed the sweep to 95,280/78,576/72,880/72,912 bytes. A main-public verifier path computes the public-segment and fixed-wiring evaluations from verifier-owned data, reaching 71,280 bytes. The next wire pass sends 15 division-free slopes per distinct 16-field initial leaf and reconstructs the omitted value from the authenticated terminal target. A public padding-fiber permutation plus inverse point action then groups natural zero padding into complete fold fibers, so the terminal wire carries 781 fields and the verifier restores 243 fixed zeros before authenticating the full committed root. The final inverse-rate-log sweep measured 70,864/66,304/**65,440**/66,512/68,208 proof bytes for logs two through six; the direct envelope is **65,452 bytes**, a transparent-wire geometry measurement. Honest verification, every public mutation, proof mutation, trailing-byte rejection, the scalar relation's 30 adversarial mutations, representative private-region mutations, a direct-public-MLE differential, 16 deterministic scalar-to-M4 differential fixtures, wrong-target rejection, and malicious omitted-coordinate rejection pass. The result is a measured Pay1x2 geometry record, not the complete production relation and not a frontier point: it omits the maximum 2-input/2-output, four-slot, stablecoin, private-authorization, all-activity-mask surface and remains transparent under upstream's 96-bit/SHA-256/GF(2^128) profile. Enabling BaseFold masking only on the trace oracle would add roughly 44,976 bytes and would still expose other witness-dependent reduction messages, so it is neither a zero-knowledge proof nor a qualifying fix. The accepted production frontier is empty. Admission requires independent source-bound certificates for the complete relation, exact binding, end-to-end zero knowledge, composed PQ128/QROM, two retained exact artifacts, and Lean/native refinement. A separate SHAKE256-512 integration produces a 149,360-byte rate-two Pay1x2 proof, but remains rejected because the transcript and algebraic profile are unchanged.
- The strict mixed-field arithmetic prototype implements the pinned GHASH B128 field and `E384 = B128[Y]/(Y^3+Y+1)` with exact 16-byte and 48-byte little-endian encodings. Independent cross-checks confirm the GHASH reduction polynomial, the cubic Frobenius irreducibility witness, multiplication, inversion, and serialization. Its mixed-IOP seam lifts B128 codewords, stores E384 values as three B128 coefficient lanes, reconstructs and folds those lanes under E384 challenges, and differentially checks multilinear evaluation. The replacement channel types fix committed symbols to B128 and algebraic challenges/claims to E384; the dependency-free transcript maps exactly 48 framed SHAKE256 bytes bijectively to each challenge. The canonical `HGMXSC01` toy exact-decodes a transparently transmitted three-lane table and runs a degree-one E384 sumcheck. Its serializer emits the counters rather than estimating them: `16 + 48*N + 64 + 96*log2(N)` bytes, including `3*N` B128 symbols, two explicit E384 claims per round, and one root; the four-value KAT is exactly 464 bytes. Transcript-derived challenges cost no wire bytes even though explicit E384 claims do. Product-ring zero-divisor and cross-term negatives prove why three componentwise B128 challenges cannot inherit field-based Schwartz-Zippel, sumcheck, or FRI reasoning. The 19 dependency-free tests pass under a lightweight direct Rust build. The full-table toy is neither compact, binding/hiding PCS evidence, nor ZK/security evidence; pinned BaseFold/FRI still has no mixed-field integration. The production gate remains empty until the real PCS/IOP, maximum-degree accounting, ZK composition, and QROM reduction are implemented and reviewed.
- Strict masking follows the E384 value domain, not merely the B128 commitment alphabet. Every mask for an E384 claim is sampled as three independent B128 coefficients and committed/authenticated as an E384 element. A one-coefficient B128 mask covers only a one-dimensional subspace; two B128 dummy rows cover at most dimension two, and an E384 witness translation outside that span produces disjoint transcript support. Therefore the same-field endpoint calculation is retained only as a B128 negative-control result. The strict simulator must separately prove full B128-linear rank for the E384 A/B endpoints and the correlated multiplication endpoint, then compose that bound with the OTP, trace, Libra, PCS, and Fiat--Shamir layers.
- The quadratic-extension alternative is executable as a separate arithmetic/transport seam. `GhashSq256b` uses the pinned relation `Y^2=X*Y+X`; two domain-separated streams share one n15 B128 table commitment but retain separate E256 challenges and claims. Thirteen direct-Rust tests and an independent manifest checker freeze the field KATs, actual table root, exact parser, and two schedule costs: 257,808 bytes for shared q=264 full paths and 512,544 bytes for the independent union. A pinned trait audit finds that low-level FRI already supports extension values over base-field twiddles, but the IP/IOP channels, BaseFold compiler, Merkle layer, and M4 integration force commitment symbols, messages, claims, and challenges through one field type. A strict compact implementation must split base-symbol commitments from extension-field algebra and serialize later E256 values as two B128 lanes; a whole-backend E256 substitution changes the input commitment and M4 geometry. Those figures intentionally do not use rate-expanded coding or compact multiproofs and therefore neither validate nor refute the lower first-level screens. Product soundness, PCS proximity, hiding, complete ZK, and QROM remain false.
- The local characteristic-two HVZK kernel masks a degree-`d` sumcheck round with a uniform element of the kernel of `p -> p(0)+p(1)`. For quadratic rounds this is `a+bX(X+1)` plus the carried `Delta*X` offset. Exhaustive small-field fibers and deterministic B128/E384 cases verify uniform visible round views and exact consistency. The canonical wire sends 48 bytes per E384 linear round and 96 bytes per quadratic round, reconstructs the omitted coefficient, and sends no terminal delta. Negative controls show TV one for missing, B128-only, or reused masks and for revealing the terminal delta; an unbound hidden terminal accepts a false sum. Therefore the kernel is used only as a local algebraic component. It is not complete ZK until a pre-challenge binding mask source and terminal relation are jointly simulated with the PCS, FRI, Merkle, outer proof, and Fiat--Shamir transform.
- The Diamond/DP24 compatibility screen parameterizes the pinned ring switch over `(B128,GhashSq256b)` and prices the actual large-field ZK BaseFold grammar rather than transferring its theorem by analogy. For n15, inverse-rate log three, fold arity two, and two independent 132-bit branches, each branch needs q=160 leaves. One salted union opening against the shared input root is 227,392 bytes; all branch-local blind/fold trees and messages bring the declared skeleton to 1,172,480 bytes. Root/authentication sharing saves 20,480 bytes, while shared query indices are rejected because they retain one proximity miss event. Diamond's theorem still excludes ZK ring switching and higher PIOP composition, so the result is a published-route no-go and not a security certificate.
- The strict-hash profile absorbs a canonical context before the public statement: pinned backend revision, inline circuit ID, HGS2 statement/circuit/crypto/backend/profile IDs, SHAKE profile, B128 modulus/basis/serialization fingerprint, framed relation-source digest, and inverse-rate log. Prover and verifier use the identical preamble; wrong-rate and prospective-E384 field-context mutations reject. Raw upstream M4 verification can omit this boundary and is therefore not release-authorized. The previously measured 149,360-byte SHAKE256-512 Pay1x2 proof predates the context-bound wire and remains a rejected transparent/B128 geometry result rather than a remeasured strict proof.
- The first full-wrapper ZK experiment appended 128 satisfiable random AND rows and produced a 317,312-byte weak-profile proof, but the construction is rejected. The appended words occupy leaves with five shared high address bits, so the trace-evaluation equality polynomial has verifier points that zero every random contribution. This defeats a universal simulator/full-rank claim and gives an honest-challenge bad event of approximately `2^-125.68` before composition. Passing roundtrip and mutation tests did not establish zero knowledge; the artifact was deleted and its measurement is marked ineligible. The structural replacement must mask each clear witness-dependent functional with a pre-challenge committed key, prove the shifted relation inside the outer proof, gate the supported clear-claim rank, and provide a simulator/statistical bound for the maximum relation.
- The first pinned-source structural trace-mask patch is retained only as a rejected artifact. Its ring-switch tensor has hypercube sum `eq_r_double_prime[0]`, not one, so committing `pi + k * 1` changes the opening by `k * eq_r_double_prime[0]`; the old `(s+k)-s-k=0` constraint is false on honest transcripts. Multiplying by the coefficient without excluding zero still leaves an annihilating transcript. The replacement method must use the exact coefficient, consume a fixed transcript-symmetric nonzero challenge rule in native/builder/replay channels, and establish a full simulator/composition argument; otherwise complete-ZK remains false.
- The coefficient-aware `M=s+k*c` source patch remains rejected by the joint simulator audit. Outer Spartan's separately serialized `precommit_claim=<K,T_K>` correlates with the inner `E=m+K` stream and exposes a witness functional. The selected repair removes that component scalar and adds a cross-oracle BaseFold Phase-A group for the precommit and private wiring contributions, while retaining independent per-oracle masks and reduced evaluations. An isolated post-coefficient patch now implements that grouped API and the single aggregate bridge across prover, builder, replay, and verifier paths; its clean-stack source checker passes and Phase B is unchanged. The exact B128 model predicts `-16` serialized bytes and clear-linear closure for every `c!=0`, but the patch is uncompiled weak-profile mechanics. No complete-ZK claim is permitted until exact multiplicities, joint PCS/FRI/Merkle and outer-Spartan simulators, nonlinear composition, QROM bounds, and the strict mixed-field realization are implemented and checked.
- The executable strict refold/PCS model charges serializer bytes, soundness terms, active relation symbols, and encoded-oracle resources separately. The verifier reconstructs the terminal checksum from the authenticated terminal vector and observes it at the unchanged Fiat--Shamir position, removing exactly one 96-byte message. Its 112,784-byte mixed-field Ligerito core is a non-ZK Pay1x2/n14 model with unfrozen active-symbol provenance. For unfrozen screens, n15 reaches 117,488 bytes only at a 32 GiB encoded oracle and rises to 133,200 bytes under a 512 MiB cap; n16 is 144,496 bytes at 64 GiB and 168,688 bytes under the cap. The retained serialized view now has an executable complete-ZK counterexample: at one fully active E384 residual coordinate, `rank(O G_r)=1` while `rank([O G_r|O G_w])=3`, giving a conditional TV-one distinguisher. Three full B128 coordinate masks close that local block only, with direct source-structure floor `96q+192` bytes before a verifier-constraint wrapper, authentication changes, adaptive whole-view simulator, or QROM composition. The companion strict mixed-PCS screen independently computes the 264-bit query count, exact worst canonical Merkle frontier, true E384/E256 lane costs, and encoded-tree storage for rates `1/2` through `1/32`, using the required 64-byte SHAKE256-512 proof digest. Four B128 symbols per leaf minimize the screened rate-`1/8` wire, reducing the first-level TensorSwitch floor from 146,656 to 136,496 bytes and tree storage from about 160 MiB to 64 MiB. A 32-byte per-leaf salt raises that wire to 140,560 bytes and storage to about 80 MiB, without by itself proving hiding. The paired-E256 rate-`1/16` and rate-`1/32` grouped-and-salted screens are 117,088 and 99,328 bytes, leaving only 6,980 and 24,740 bytes for every later round and ZK message. E256 x 2 remains fail-closed until a parallel RBR/extraction theorem, characteristic-two ZK ring switch, complete simulator, and composed QROM reduction exist. Neither model records a production candidate and `strict_admitted` remains false.
- The random-padding branch separates its fixed-matrix lemma from the actual protocol. The Lean and executable models prove the exact B128-linear range/rank criterion and its zero-rank and reused-pad failures. The source integration fills only whole unused B128 trace symbols and feeds the same randomized buffer to shift reduction and commitment, but it also exposes that shift folding is generally F2-linear rather than B128-linear. A complete audit therefore needs binary-basis coefficient maps for every shift, ring-switch, BaseFold, query, and terminal observation, bound to the verifier/replay schedule; the old one-B128-coefficient-row abstraction is not sufficient. The executable SHAKE256-512/E256 FRI toy exports 104 rows, finds padding rank four versus joint rank eight, and is explicitly non-ZK. At n15 the actual separate-tree one-round q=33 wire is 118,192 bytes and the maximum-overlap combined-tree screen is 89,744 bytes, but fifteen degree-halving rounds have a 325,424-byte structural floor. Exact per-tree classical salt pricing raises that floor to 387,427 bytes and still does not match the fixed 512-bit digest parameter or supply a QROM simulator. A STIR screen is also a no-go: 403,392 canonical bytes, and 128,456 bytes even with authentication nodes deleted. The conditional two-branch product identity is recorded, but the protocol lacks the required history-conditional extraction and ROM/QROM premises. All former 69,632/128,512 and 63,320/116,952 rows are precursor screens rather than proof sizes.
- The historical VOLE/MPC-in-the-head screen expands every native M4 word-AND into its 64 Boolean products. Even the incomplete 83-Keccak source has 3,187,200 Boolean multiplications, not 49,800 field multiplications; the source omitted both full ciphertext hashes and is not the V6 target. Primary VOLEitH communication at 16 bits per Boolean multiplication is 6,374,400 bytes; the 99,600-byte word-level result is an explicit zero-divisor reward-hacking negative control. Its best checkpoint model is still far above the compact target and has no complete-ZK/QROM theorem, M4 refinement, or production verifier.
- The binding-vector model now separates its executable affine toy from the nonlinear maximum relation. Full M4 has 3,187,200 BitAnd constraints, and a masked AND needs the three cross terms induced by `(a+r_a)(b+r_b)`. The exact screened `[trace || mask]` grammar therefore remains unimplemented; its optimistic joint-opening search is already 288,408 raw bytes at n15 under the 28-GiB oracle cap, while two E256 repetitions are 280,056 bytes. The earlier 113,904-byte affine serializer is never a candidate, and even its unsound all-terminal-elision control does not establish binding, hiding, complete ZK, or strict security.
- The historical maximum scalar SHAKE model fixes `value_balance` to zero, exhaustively accepts only the nine masks with at least one input and one output, rejects the other seven, and requires nonzero enabled stablecoin issuance. `note.cm3` commits a private `NoteKind`; its five modes restrict which kinds can be created and consumed and enforce approval lineage. This is finite semantic evidence only. The V6 executable non-hash relation, fixed statement, state transition, stablecoin gate, and Lean/native refinement must independently enforce the same intended rules.
- The rejected pre-V6 maximum-relation action method reconstructed an 853-byte statement and appended a verifier-derived intent digest through `FullProofBinding` v3. Although its host adapter checked active 2,147-byte wallet ciphertexts, inactive outputs carried no fixed relation slot and the M4 relation omitted both full ciphertext hash calls. Its 853-byte grammar, 912-byte public transport, and `HGSP` envelope are historical fixtures, not a successor statement or admission interface.
- The rejected source-level maximum M4 realization used one main circuit, 671 witness words, 114 verifier-owned public words, and an 83-Keccak-f schedule. It omitted the two fixed ciphertext hash calls, relied on composition for public hashes, and is not the 79-invocation/124-permutation V6 target. Its source geometry is retained only as a no-go input to architecture comparisons.
- The retained SHAKE256-400 M4 envelope is **1,344,828 bytes** (`3 * 448,224`), SHA-256 `0ce525a6c790e1be4c47cf6d482b767f455616f06e5ab013d561378d6ab81a56`. Its restart/parser/mutation/fresh-replay checks pass for the historical 853-byte/83-permutation source, but that source is incomplete, its 133.332424-bit value is only an arithmetic screen, the serial direct-product QROM theorem is open, and the adaptive simulator is open. It is negative retained evidence, not a production-frontier or V6 proof artifact.
- The allocation-free geometry counter pins the M4 source, Keccak gadget and compiler fusion assumptions. The live source uses direct packed-public loads and cached balance values/selectors, replaces the Merkle mask/delta cone by one MSB select plus XOR recovery of the other child, uses the exact five-mode one-hot partition to remove redundant SHAKE-frame mux gates, and validates one selected policy structure while preserving both approval-state lanes. Four reverse-checked frozen patches, 262,247 total deterministic checks, semantic-boundary source pins, and independent audits tighten the conservative syntactic upper from 65,336 to 60,788 hidden words, at most 30,394 active B128 symbols, and at least 2,374 unused n15 symbols. The full 1,984-symbol screen has 390 symbols of source-static margin. This is not a compiled-size reduction: compiler CSE may already collapse part of the first duplicate graph, and fusion/DCE can change the later deltas. Every rank and byte result remains conditional until typecheck, compiled statistics, and scalar/M4 differential execution freeze the exact active prefix. An n16 compile invalidates the n15 parameters.
- The historical `Hegemon.FullShakeRelation` model mechanizes the old 853-byte grammar's finite mask, mode, balance, stablecoin, and lineage tables without manufacturing cryptographic certificates. It is useful finite differential evidence but not arbitrary-input acceptance equivalence and not the V6 893-byte grammar. Its SHAKE, Rust/M4 refinement, complete-ZK, algebra, PQ128/QROM, and deployed-verifier premises remain uninhabited; its old byte screens are not candidates.
- The retained SmallWood output row-boundary slice proves its named V4 commitment/ciphertext-row and inactive-field theorems, and focused regressions mutate the corresponding legacy rows and public fields. Those twelve scalar ciphertext-hash rows close a V4 free-field gap but hash only a host-supplied digest; they do not constrain the full 2,147-byte ciphertext through SHAKE and cannot authorize V6. Full SmallWood/AIR/PCS soundness and conventional-hash source refinement remain open.
- The consensus version-policy kernel proves initial bindings, activation/retirement boundary behavior, same-height retirement precedence, duplicate initial binding deduplication, and first unsupported transaction ordering with `Hegemon.Consensus.versionPolicy_initial_accepts` and the companion rejection theorems. `gen_version_policy_vectors` emits examples that Rust `VersionSchedule::validate_versions` checks on the PoW and BFT import paths, and the blueprint binds the PoW/BFT wrapper calls before proof verification, node insertion, vote-history mutation, slashing mutation, or fork mutation. This does not prove social upgrade governance, manifest correctness, per-version proof-system soundness, or native-node equivalence.
- The bridge message-root transcript grammar is part of the Lean bridge slice. `Hegemon.Bridge.MessageRoot` proves representative facts for exact root preimage domain/count/hash length-prefix bytes, invalid hash-length rejection, ordered-pair transcript construction, reversed-pair distinction, and count-prefix binding. `gen_bridge_vectors` emits those transcript examples alongside bridge encoding/replay examples, and the Rust `protocol-kernel` conformance test checks them against the production root preimage helper. This does not prove BLAKE3 cryptographic security or implementation equivalence.
- The bridge long-range proof-shape admission table is part of the Lean bridge slice. `Hegemon.Bridge.LongRange` proves representative facts for verifier-hash binding, message-count binding, header/MMR length shape, trusted/message/tip height ordering, selected message source, sample-domain size, duplicate sample-index rejection, sample height/opening-index agreement, confirmation policy, tip-work policy, and claimed-output agreement. `gen_bridge_long_range_vectors` emits examples that the Rust `consensus-light-client` conformance test checks against the production helper called by the long-range verifier. The production full and guest wire decoders are additionally checked against an independent raw-byte oracle for exact consumption, truncation, non-canonical compact lengths, allocation caps, guest output-tail mismatch, and deterministic valid-wire mutations; this is parser-equivalence hardening, not yet a Lean-modeled wire grammar. This does not prove SHA-256 implementation equivalence, PoW hash security, MMR hash binding, complete long-range proof wire-grammar equivalence, probabilistic FlyClient sampling soundness, or complete bridge light-client soundness.
- The bridge checkpoint-output binding kernel is part of the Lean bridge slice. `Hegemon.Bridge.CheckpointOutput` proves representative facts for the exact output domain, canonical-preimage/domain-plus-wire relation, fixed 436-byte journal tuple, trusted-checkpoint digest field, scalar little-endian encodings, and max-scalar cases. `gen_bridge_checkpoint_output_vectors` emits canonical and journal byte examples that the Rust `consensus-light-client` conformance test checks against production output encoding and decoding helpers, including fixed-length rejection for truncated or trailing journal bytes. This does not prove BLAKE3 cryptographic security or implementation equivalence, bridge message-root/hash derivation, external verifier behavior, or complete bridge light-client soundness.
- The bridge header-MMR opening-shape kernel is part of the Lean bridge slice. `Hegemon.Bridge.HeaderMmr` proves representative facts for peak decomposition, context mismatch rejection, leaf bounds, sibling-count admission, local-index derivation, and left/right path orientation. `gen_bridge_header_mmr_vectors` emits examples that the Rust `consensus-light-client` conformance test checks against `evaluate_header_mmr_opening_shape`, the helper used by standalone and long-range MMR opening verification. This does not prove SHA-256 implementation equivalence, MMR parent/root hash security, FlyClient sampling soundness, or complete bridge light-client soundness.
- The bridge header-MMR parent/root transcript kernel is part of the Lean bridge slice. `Hegemon.Bridge.HeaderMmrTranscript` proves representative facts for exact parent/root domains, u32/u64 little-endian counters, left/right child order, ordered peak concatenation, empty roots, and reversed-peak distinction. `gen_bridge_header_mmr_transcript_vectors` emits examples that the Rust `consensus-light-client` conformance test checks against `header_mmr_parent_preimage_v1` and `header_mmr_root_preimage_v1`, the helpers hashed by MMR construction and verification. This does not prove BLAKE3 cryptographic security or implementation equivalence, SHA-256 header-hash implementation equivalence, MMR collision resistance, full MMR hash binding, FlyClient sampling soundness, or complete bridge light-client soundness.
- The bridge FlyClient sampling transcript/index kernel is part of the Lean bridge slice. `Hegemon.Bridge.FlyClient` proves representative facts for the exact sample transcript domain and little-endian range/sample-counter byte layout, digest-prefix modulo reduction, duplicate sample preservation at the raw sampler layer, sample-count truncation, and empty/reversed ranges. The long-range admission table separately requires a large enough sample domain and rejects duplicate sample indices before counting the sample floor. `gen_bridge_flyclient_vectors` emits examples that the Rust `consensus-light-client` conformance test checks against the production helpers used by `flyclient_sample_indices`. This does not prove BLAKE3 cryptographic security or implementation equivalence, MMR hash binding, probabilistic FlyClient sampling soundness, or complete bridge light-client soundness.
- Every production-eligible blueprint node must name at least one cheap falsification case, such as an invalid vector, negative unit test, counterexample config, or checker case that would fail quickly if the claim were false. Every checked-in target-review record remains `needs_review` or `blocked`; the source-bound digest detects edits to the node, implementation/evidence paths, and referenced source bytes, but it is not a signature and does not authorize the proposition. Independent review must make any acceptance decision outside this self-authored ledger, and no review status changes a claim's residual risks.
- Blueprint implementation call-order and dominance constraints carry `lean_theorems` references. Formal-core validates those references against the node's claims-ledger `lean_theorems`, reports theorem-indexed order/dominance coverage, and the source-of-record blueprint enables `require_theorem_indexed_order_constraints`, so production order gates fail closed if they are only Rust-call-graph indexed. Current coverage is 154/154 order constraints and 139/139 dominance constraints theorem-indexed. The remaining work is semantic refinement: narrow broad claim-level theorem lists to the smallest theorem set that actually justifies each specific order gate, and keep that discipline for new admission, replay, sidecar, proof, storage, and network publication gates.
- A local pass is preflight evidence. Branch acceptance requires the CI `formal-core` job to run `bash scripts/check_formal_core.sh` and report the blueprint-DAG step passed. The release aggregate also requires explicit `formal-crypto-isolation` and complete `formal-crypto-sanity` jobs, and the latter runs the exact Lean-generated proof-wire regression against the production Rust parser and active-artifact verifier. Tag releases rerun those three checks inside `security-gates` before native review-package verification. The release workflow wiring gate additionally checks that dependency-audit runs the explicit waiver gate, formal-core, security-adversarial, native-backend-security, app-no-ssh-e2e, release-build dependencies, the all-binary PQ audit for `hegemon-node`/`wallet`/`walletd`, and tag-release native backend review/posture gates are present. `Hegemon.Release.CiReleaseGate.accepted_ci_release_gate_depends_on_dependency_audit_policy` exposes dependency-audit, dependency-waiver gate, release-build, and release-build security dependency facts from accepted CI policy, while `accepted_ci_release_gate_exposes_required_policy_facts` also exposes the app no-SSH E2E job and release-build dependency. Passing these gates is release-gate evidence, not a proof of the full Rust implementation and not a replacement for TLC/Apalache runs, external cryptanalysis, or checked-in acceptance artifacts.
- `circuits/formal/README.md` and `consensus/spec/formal/README.md` explain how to run the TLA+ models. Include the TLC/Apalache output summary in PR descriptions when those specs change; set `HEGEMON_FORMAL_RUN_MODEL_CHECKERS=1` before running `scripts/check_formal_core.sh` if local TLC/Apalache binaries are installed and you want the wrapper to run them too.
- `runbooks/security_testing.md` documents how to rerun the `security-adversarial` job locally, capture artifacts, and notify auditors if a regression appears on CI. That job installs the pinned Lean toolchain manager, and `scripts/run_proving_redteam.sh` requires `lake` up front because its mandatory SmallWood campaign generates Lean-owned conformance vectors. Treat the runbook as mandatory reading before release tagging.
- Run `./scripts/dependency-audit-gate.sh` for dependency advisories; CI and release builds fail on any unwaived finding, any stale waiver that no longer matches a current finding, and any waiver missing owner, reviewed_at, remediation, expiry, reason, or DEP-format tracking metadata. `Hegemon.Release.DependencyAuditPolicy.accepted_dependency_audit_waiver_release_metadata_fields`, `accepted_dependency_audit_waiver_has_current_review_policy`, `accepted_dependency_audit_finding_has_explicit_current_reviewed_waiver`, and `accepted_dependency_audit_waiver_has_live_exact_finding` expose the field-level metadata, expiry/review flags, exact per-finding waiver witness, and exact live-finding witness from accepted dependency audit policy, while `Hegemon.Release.ReleasePostureCertificate.production_release_gate_certificate_binds_dependency_audit_to_ci_release_gate` carries the accepted dependency audit into the production release CI binding. Use `./scripts/dependency-audit.sh --record` only to append a human-readable snapshot after updating `config/dependency-audit-waivers.json`.

### Formal transcript/profile conformance

- The SmallWood transcript/profile binding slice proves the exact V4/Gamma byte layout exposed by the shipped transcript helper: SHA-512 Level-5 domains, verifier-profile material, `DirectPacked64CompressedLevel5`, the 23-opening `2^20` DECS profile, serialized public statement bytes, and canonical padding. `gen_smallwood_transcript_binding_vectors` emits Lean-derived cases for the active profile, statement mutation, version/profile mutation, every recognized historical arithmetization tag, padding boundaries, and all 16 active-profile single-field mutations. The production `transaction-circuit` gate reconstructs each in-range mutation, requires exact Lean byte agreement, proves the corresponding Rust profile array differs at exactly the named index, requires separation from the active profile, and rejects duplicate or missing mutation names. `HegemonCrypto.SmallWood.ProductionAcceptanceClosure` proves properties of a caller-supplied verifier-evidence record; `HegemonCrypto.SmallWood.ProductionSupplyChain` composes ordered transaction identity, fees, coinbase, and claimed supply only after caller-supplied extraction and semantic-refinement evidence. The ideal-QROM theorem is not a consumer in that chain. Primitive SHA-512/Poseidon2 security, deployed hash-instantiation loss, the production semantic bridge, and complete arbitrary Rust/compiler/machine refinement remain explicit assumptions.
- `Hegemon.Transaction.ProofSystemBoundary.accepted_canonical_statement_surface_with_balance_soundness_implies_proof_artifact_admission_statement_certificate` is the proof-artifact admission certificate for the canonical transaction statement surface. It composes an accepted canonical statement surface with the existing balance-soundness assumption and exposes wrapper preconditions, wrapper surface facts, proof-wrapper metadata projection, public binding validity, public shape validity, statement preimage/length, proof binding-message construction, core/vector statement binding, balance artifact facts, and native delta authorization on one statement witness. `Hegemon.Transaction.ProofWrapperWire` now adds raw outer-wrapper bincode fixtures for canonical, trailing, truncated, malformed, invalid-backend, and raw-to-admission top-level-nullifier-drift `TransactionProof` bytes; `gen_proof_wrapper_wire_vectors` schema-v2 checks them against production exact decode, canonical reserialization, decoded admission rejection, and the arbitrary-byte bincode/full-consumption/canonical-reencode mutation corpus. `Hegemon.Transaction.SmallWoodRecursiveEnvelopeWire` adds the matching recursive proof-envelope parser/descriptor handoff table for valid profile-A/profile-B envelopes, trailing/truncated/invalid-enum/truncated-proof-vector rejection, and descriptor mismatch before recursive proof verification. This is proof-only tightening; it does not discharge arbitrary bincode/postcard correctness, STARK/FRI/PCS soundness, verifier implementation equivalence, witness extraction, or hash security.

### Documentation + threat-model synchronization

Whenever you touch an API, threat mitigation, or performance assumption:

1. Update the component README (e.g., `wallet/README.md`) with the new commands or invariants.
2. Update `docs/API_REFERENCE.md` so integrators can find the function signatures.
3. Update `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` when security margins move.
4. Reflect the architectural impact in `DESIGN.md §6` (or the relevant subsystem section) and record the operational/testing changes here in METHODS.
5. Mention the change in `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` so future contributors know which CI jobs/benchmarks cover it.

PRs missing any of these sync points should be blocked during review; CI surfaces the changed docs alongside code so reviewers can verify everything moved together.

### Aggregation authoring update (February 27, 2026)

- Local prover execution now uses bounded reusable work queues instead of per-job cold starts. This keeps recursion artifacts on stable worker threads and reduces repeated cache rebuilds caused by thread churn.
- Aggregation cache warmup is now explicit and checkpointed by default:
  - `HEGEMON_AGG_PREWARM_MAX_TXS` controls whether breadth warmup is attempted at all (unset defaults to no automatic max-target expansion on the hot path).
  - `HEGEMON_AGG_PREWARM_MODE=checkpoint` (default) expands warmup shapes geometrically (`1,2,4,8,...`) when a max tx cap is provided.
  - `HEGEMON_AGG_PREWARM_MODE=linear` restores legacy linear warmup.
  - `HEGEMON_AGG_WARMUP_TARGET_SHAPES` continues to support explicit shape lists.

Operationally, this change removes hidden O(target) warmup churn from live proving and makes warmup policy explicit in runbooks and benchmark configs.

### Native mining history access (August 30, 2026)

Native work preparation, mining status, and mined-work revalidation derive the header-history commitment from the canonical state's compact MMR peaks. They perform zero active full-chain reconstruction and do not decode historical block bodies. Every difficulty calculation first exact-matches the supplied parent against its stored record. Between retargets it needs no older row. At a retarget boundary it follows the stored parent-hash ancestry through at most `RETARGET_WINDOW - 1` bounded PoW metadata projections; the canonical path additionally proves that the supplied parent and the reached retarget anchor are the hashes in their respective canonical height-index rows. Thus the optimization is a bounded stored-parent ancestry walk followed by indexed canonical binding, not a claim that a retarget needs only one anchor read. A stale parent, missing record or height row, broken parent link, inconsistent ancestor height, or mismatched indexed anchor rejects before schedule data is used. The peak cache is derived from canonical state and is not a replacement for persisted block authority: a missing persisted tip, an inconsistent ancestor, or a malformed peak count rejects. The hot-path helper can cheaply validate only that the number of peaks has the shape implied by the leaf count; it cannot detect same-shape peak-content corruption. Peak-content integrity is therefore a derived-state lifecycle invariant enforced by startup reconstruction and canonical append/reorg publication, with reopen regressions comparing the exact peaks and next work header against the full-history oracle. Deterministic native-node regressions compare `pow_bits`, header-MMR root and length, and the prepared header preimage with the legacy full-chain oracle while also counting metadata loads and full-chain reconstructions. Any mismatch is a consensus regression; the optimization is permitted only when accepted blocks and header bytes remain identical.

A non-tip announced block and a noncanonical sync page first walk stored ancestry as compact PoW/header projections, then replay stored block bodies one at a time while carrying only the consensus state needed by the next block. A winning non-tip announcement durability-flushes its newly validated content-addressed row before adoption. A sync response exact-classifies every supplied row into the logical tri-state `KnownExact`, `Missing`, or terminal same-hash metadata mismatch. The ordinary known-prefix/all-missing canonical-tip extension keeps its atomic fast path. A mixed `KnownExact / Missing / KnownExact` suffix instead validates and replays the complete connected supplied suffix before one sled batch persists only the missing rows. The terminal mismatch path performs no write. This prevents an already-stored descendant after a missing connector from being misclassified as an unstored overwrite or trapped in a same-range retry.

Winning fork adoption never retains a `Vec<NativeBlockMeta>` for the replacement chain. After the unknown rows are durability-flushed, reorganization rebuilds a compact height/hash/parent/PoW ancestry, exact-checks genesis and every durable row under the state write lock, and performs separate one-stored-body-at-a-time passes for action hashes, old-branch orphan recovery, consensus replay, canonical-index planning, and staged-ciphertext removal. Replay releases the winning tip's action/signature payload before later body passes and reloads the exact durable tip just before commit. The persistence admission gate requires connected exact rows, complete suffix validation, the classified missing count to equal the noncanonical batch writes, a crossed durability barrier, a complete durable replacement, and `canonical_transaction_block_record_writes = 0`. The canonical sled transaction then replaces the height/canonical-state/pending indexes and best pointer before publication. This removes chain-depth-proportional block-body ownership from active announced/sync reorganization, but it is not a constant-memory claim: replay state, action-hash/orphan/pending/staged collections, the complete canonical output plan (including ciphertext bytes), height rows, and atomic transaction key collections remain proportional to history, state, or output size.

Startup similarly reconstructs the canonical metadata chain once as part of the existing block-index validation gate. Only after that validation succeeds does `NativeNode::open` borrow the non-cloneable snapshot for bridge replay validation, exact header-MMR peak derivation, canonical state replay, and ciphertext index/archive verification; the snapshot is released before open returns. The loaded-state, repair, and publication order is unchanged. Replay and canonical-index planning still make separate streaming passes over action bodies, and the one snapshot remains proportional to chain height for the duration of startup, so this is a repeated-allocation reduction rather than a constant-memory startup claim. A thread-local structural regression counts the lowest-level chain loader and decoded metadata rows across reopen and requires exactly one load and one decoded row per canonical height.

Auto-recursive candidate actions duplicate artifact bytes in both the route `public_args` and the embedded `candidate_artifact`. The bounded same-tip cache remains useful while the canonical parent is unchanged, but every successful mined, announced-tip, sync-tip-batch, or reorg state publication clears it immediately. Already-known, missing-parent, nonwinning, and failed imports do not clear it because they publish no new canonical state.

Scalar and header-only native RPCs read `best_height`, `best_hash`, `pow_bits`, or the compact best header directly and do not clone the tip's `action_bytes`. `chain_getBlock` and other body-returning or bounded row RPCs still decode the requested persisted record because their response includes body data; the optimization is therefore removal of avoidable tip-body clones, not a claim that every RPC is allocation-free.

### Native fork-sync recovery (August 30, 2026)

Sync import records four materially different outcomes: canonical advancement, storage of a valid nonwinning side-branch block, an already-known block, and a missing parent. Missing-parent is a recovery signal and must never be folded into already-known success. Stop importing the response at that gap, preserve the unresolved hash-anchored target, and request a bounded ancestor window that includes both a locally verified anchor and the peer branch's first unknown child. The anchor row is context, not a second canonical import. A 64-block response is a required regression size because it reproduces the live failure in which a child-only `43,530..43,593` response omitted the divergent parent at `43,529`.

After a nonempty response, compare the canonical best hash/height before and after import. Reset ancestor backfill only after canonical progress. For a missing parent, exponentially widen the bounded backfill window and then page adjacent capped windows backward until a locally verified anchor is found. Once a response connects to known storage, including an all-known or stored-noncanonical prefix, advance the side-branch frontier from the verified response tip toward the advertised target. A nonempty response must be a contiguous prefix of the exact outstanding request; suffix-only, gap, duplicate-height, wrong-parent, and unsolicited responses reject before chain reconstruction. Only `InFlight` requests authorize responses; cooldown entries suppress late duplicates without authorizing import. Persist the peer-bound recovery cursor across ordinary expiry and monotone same-peer target growth, bind a forward page's first parent to the preceding response tip, and require the announced target hash when a response reaches the advertised target height. Equal-height ancestor pages remain recovery evidence rather than stale-tip or target-resolution evidence. Empty, malformed, stale, busy-import, worker-failure, or import-error replies cool down the exact completed request instead of immediately issuing it again. A compact `AnnounceTip` and a full `Announce` that returns `MissingParent` remain peer-bound unverified hash hints, so neither may open the mining gate. Supplied cumulative work and pre-import response fields are not target authority. A nonwinning target may clear only after the authorized response is fully processed, the exact target row is loaded from durable local storage, and local fork choice confirms that stored row does not beat the canonical tip. Forged, mismatched, failed, or missing-parent responses keep the target unresolved and the gate closed. On timeout, invalid/empty terminal response, or chunk-session expiry, compare the complete `(height, peer, hash, provenance)` tuple, evict only an exact still-unverified match, clear its request and recovery cursor, and retain the offender in a bounded 1,024-entry map for a 20-second cooldown. The cooled peer cannot immediately reacquire the target and a lower honest target may become eligible. This is per-peer anti-pinning, not Sybil resistance, quorum proof, or validation of another peer's target. Already-known rows and stored noncanonical rows do not by themselves open the mining gate. Response count, wire-frame size, import ordering, outstanding-request state, retry/backfill state, and unverified-target cooldown state remain capped. These request/import bounds do not by themselves prove a bound on complete canonical reorganization replay or sled/allocator internals.

Steady outbound catch-up pagination uses the same per-peer four-request/ten-second budget as response serving. The first over-budget page is retained locally for a full ten seconds from the pacing decision, then the two-second scheduler retries it before the existing twenty-second request lease expires. Repeated scheduler ticks or announcements do not refresh the paced timestamp; a stopped scheduler still reaches ordinary unverified-target eviction, peer cooldown, and failover. A broadcast bootstrap request is rebound to its first authorized responder before pagination, and that authorized normal or chunk-fallback response conservatively charges the responder's first client slot at completion, so later pages neither bypass nor trail the server's per-peer budget. A requester restart can still encounter one bounded silent server rejection before its local window history converges.

Announcement-triggered requests, like periodic retries, prefer the persisted
recovery page for the exact admitted peer/height/hash tuple and retain its
expected parent and recovery classification. Repeated announcements cannot
replace a paced forward page with an ordinary near-tip/backfill request.
Stale or foreign observations do not invoke the mutating cursor lookup;
existing target admission, expiry, same-peer growth and exact-hash failover
rules remain authoritative. Preserving a cursor does not authorize a response
before its request becomes `InFlight`.

Outbound response construction applies the wire budget while reading canonical rows, rather than materializing the entire requested range and truncating it afterward. It normally retains the largest height-ordered prefix below the soft encrypted-frame target; if the first block alone exceeds that soft target but still fits the 16 MiB encrypted transport cap, it retains that single block to preserve progress. It computes the exact encoded length without allocating a second serialized frame and stops after inspecting at most one candidate beyond the retained prefix. A global service cap currently permits at most two response-building workers across all peers; duplicate ranges from one peer remain deduplicated and additional distinct work is refused until capacity returns.

If an inline announcement or range response cannot fit the transport budget, the service emits `AnnounceLocator` or `ResponseLocators` rather than a partial or empty metadata-record response. The receiver requests one exact body at a time with `BlockBodyRequest`; the sender answers with bounded `BlockBodyChunk` messages. Locator admission checks active schema, chain, rules, identities, exact body length, body hash, and derived chunk count before reservation. Completion checks exact total length and hash before bounded full-consumption decode, canonical re-encoding, locator equality, and the existing authorized import path.

`NativeSyncMessage` keeps legacy postcard ordinals 0 through 3 byte-for-byte. `AnnounceLocator`, `ResponseLocators`, `BlockBodyRequest`, and `BlockBodyChunk` are the active exact carrier at ordinals 4 through 7. `RequestBlockChunk` and `BlockChunk` occupy ordinals 8 and 9 but are rejected by `prefilter_native_sync_wire_tag` before postcard allocation because their stored-record grammar is incompatible with the exact locator/action-body model. `AnnounceTip = 10` remains a compact, unverified scheduling hint. Neither `HNW1` nor `NATIVE_SYNC_PROTOCOL_ID` performs capability negotiation, so mixed-version large-block transfer is claimed only between peers supporting the exact carrier.

Active body reassembly binds the authenticated peer and exact locator, requires a contiguous chunk index sequence, derives every chunk length from the locator total, and caps per-peer/global reassembly count, reserved bytes, idle lifetime, and absolute lifetime. Duplicate, missing, conflicting, cross-block, oversized, malformed, or hash-mismatched chunks fail before import. The separate `SyncBlockChunkAdmission` record-fallback state machine is retained only as a conditional model and test surface; it is not reachable from production wire decode and cannot authorize storage, import, or gate changes.

Completion consolidates the bounded exact body chunks once, verifies the locator-bound body hash before decode, exact-decodes and canonically re-encodes the body, and checks every locator field before handing the block to the existing response/import machinery. Until that handoff no partial body can import, claim canonical progress, or open the gate. Completion can transiently own the wire frame, retained chunks, consolidated bytes, decoded body, and canonical re-encoding; sled pages, replay state, output plans, and allocator residency remain outside the raw-body cap.

Persistence is batch-shaped only after replay acceptance. For a connected nonwinning response, replay all supplied blocks in order against carried candidate state first; only then evaluate one count-parameterized noncanonical manifest whose declared block-record writes equal the exact classified-missing count, apply one sled `Batch` containing only those missing records, and require the durability barrier before returning the stored count. For a contiguous canonical tip extension, replay each block against carried candidate state first; then evaluate one `TipExtensionBatchCommit` manifest, commit the chunk with one sled transaction, and require the durability barrier before verifying/publishing the new canonical state. The zero-missing case performs no block-record mutation and requires no new-row durability claim. These gates establish the modeled admission, ordering, and fail-closed wiring. They do not establish sled batch/transaction atomicity, operating-system fsync semantics, hash-key uniqueness, arbitrary raw block-replay soundness, or convergence for an arbitrary deep fork.

### Memory evidence and controlled validation

Report memory evidence in separate categories:

- **Measured:** process RSS/private-anonymous residency and high-water mark, OOM records, elapsed observation interval, chain height/progress, and workload. Record whether an RPC such as `hegemon_miningStatus` was polled, because the pre-fix method itself traversed history and perturbs the baseline.
- **Confirmed from source or instrumentation:** ownership and clone sites, full-chain reconstruction counts, metadata-read counts, queue/cache cardinality, and values deliberately retained across an iteration.
- **Inferred:** allocator arena/high-water retention or fragmentation after temporary values are dropped. Do not call this a confirmed live-object leak without heap-profiler reachability evidence.

The read-only live diagnosis measured database footprints of about 742 MB on `hegemon-dev` and 1.1 GB on `hegemon-ovh`, alongside much larger anonymous-RSS observations. That size contrast is operational evidence only: database bytes do not identify decoded live objects, allocator arenas, or heap reachability.

The controlled evidence combines a 65-block fork-recovery fixture built from two in-process, storage-backed `NativeNode` instances, a signed equal-height side-page/adoption integration, a pure planner regression that crosses the 255-block recovery horizon, synthetic retarget/history/startup counters, an oversized valid block-record transfer requiring more than four authenticated 4 MiB chunks, and a disposable release-node work/status/mined-import RSS soak. The chunk regressions also pin legacy ordinals 0 through 3, exercise the appended 4/5/6 ordinals, require invalid request/chunk floods to consume zero serve or receive workers, and cover malformed schema, digest, order/offset, chunk-size, and total-size failures plus idle/hard expiry, peer failover, and exact-close release. The in-process fixtures exercise production storage/import/scheduling helpers but are not a real P2P deployment, and the retarget fixture crosses schedule boundaries rather than simulating the live chain height. None of these reproduces the live 43,529-block reorganization depth; the compact/body-streaming reorganization still owns history/state/output-sized residuals, so an authorized one-node canary remains required before any claim about live-host peak memory. Controlled acceptance must show canonical forward progress, no repeated identical no-progress range, zero full-chain reconstruction in the canonical mining hot path, the documented bounded retarget-ancestry metadata-read ceiling, and a process-RSS plateau over repeated cycles. Structural counters demonstrate path complexity; the RSS soak is operational evidence. Neither is a universal proof that all node paths are leak-free or a fixed-RSS proof at live depth.

Run `./scripts/check_native_node_memory_sync_recovery.sh --structural-only` for the one-snapshot startup reload, deterministic mining/non-tip history counters and consensus oracle, exact 64-block missing-parent recovery, equal-height and monotone multi-window backward/forward recovery, branch-bound exact-request/cooldown state, atomic valid/invalid side-page reopen, streaming wire-prefix loading, global response-worker capacity, append-only sync wire ordinals, oversized-record chunk progress, invalid-flood zero-worker admission, expiry/failover/close release, chunk malformed/digest/order/oversize rejection, wire publication, and mining-gate-reopening regressions. Formal-core additionally checks the Lean-generated chunk fallback, session capacity/expiry, serve-request, canonical full/final per-chunk shape, completion, and outbound-transition admission vectors against the production helpers. A controlled local-node acceptance run uses the same script with an explicit `--pid`, loopback `--rpc-url`, `--output` CSV, and precommitted `--max-rss-growth-kib` and `--max-rss-envelope-kib` limits. Full mode warms the process, repeatedly polls the read-only mining-status path, records canonical height/hash and OS memory counters, requires active ungated nonsyncing mining plus forward height progress, and writes a JSON summary beside the CSV. The limit values are host/test policy inputs chosen before the run, not thresholds inferred from the resulting samples; the summary records that status polling was part of the workload and repeats the heap-reachability and caller-supplied PID-to-RPC-association limitations.

Before any authorized testnet rollout, build and test the exact candidate artifact, snapshot the database and service configuration, capture a read-only preflight, and stage a rollback binary/config. Set `HEGEMON_SEEDS="hegemon.pauli.group:30333,devnet.hegemonprotocol.com:30333"` unless the approved seed list has deliberately rotated; all miners on the network must share the same list to avoid partitions and forks. Keep NTP or chrony enabled because future-skewed PoW timestamps are rejected. The append-only chunk extension has no capability negotiation: before canarying an upgraded `hegemon-dev` against an old `hegemon-ovh`, verify that every record needed for catch-up fits the legacy 16 MiB frame or provide an upgraded relay. Any required record above that cap needs upgraded endpoints on both sides of its transfer. Roll one node at a time and require bounded-memory and canonical-progress evidence before proceeding; deployment or service restart remains a separate operator authorization.
Known canonical and nonwinning block-body locator claims are classified through compact verified indexes and an authenticated noncanonical fork summary without loading their action bodies. A plausible winning stored branch alone proceeds through the fair blocking import permit, where the full persisted body is exact-compared before fork-choice promotion; a near-maximum-body regression pins zero full-body loads for the compact classification path. Multi-block sync reorgs consume their bounded caller-supplied contiguous prefix from the last reloaded canonical ancestor, then run the unchanged full suffix replay and atomic publication gates; unverified intermediate blocks are never written only to satisfy parent traversal.

### Historical SmallWood V5 candidate-boundary verification (rejected)

The conventional-hash V5 work is a retained parser and negative-authorization boundary, not a proof-system, security result, or current candidate. These historical allocation-light checks demonstrate that V5/Delta remains inactive:

```sh
python3 -B .agent/hardening/smallwood-pqc-zk/test_strict_profile.py
python3 -B scripts/test_check_smallwood_v5_candidate_gate.py
python3 -B scripts/check_smallwood_v5_candidate_gate.py
python3 -B scripts/check_smallwood_v5_candidate_gate.py --require-authorized
```

The first two commands must pass their positive and adversarial fixtures. The third must report `posture=inactive`, and the fourth must fail. No completion of this historical checklist may authorize V5 under a fresh successor identity.

When Cargo workspace discovery is healthy, run the focused Rust gates:

```sh
cargo test -p protocol-versioning
cargo test -p transaction-circuit smallwood_blake2b384_semantics --lib
cargo test -p transaction-circuit smallwood_v5_envelope --lib
cargo test -p protocol-kernel --test smallwood_v5_inactive
```

The BLAKE2b relation test pins a partial 77-call reference schedule across the old statement adapter, stablecoin and private-authorization modes, and public/auth/Merkle mutations. Its exact negative geometry is 15,065 framed bytes, 164 compression blocks, 16,322,454 scalar constraints/witness bits, and 255,100 packed-64 rows before non-hash logic. It omitted the two full 2,147-byte ciphertext hash calls, so it is neither complete nor the V6 target. The envelope tests retain the exact historical `80 + 672 + proof` grammar and byte-identical transport negative controls. The kernel test requires V5/Delta and action 7 to remain absent from every active manifest and backend mapping. V6 uses a fresh identity, statement, relation-descriptor binding, and envelope and must not inherit any V5 authorization.

### HGV8RP03 semantic-refinement method

Run `lake build Hegemon.Transaction.Poseidon2V8SemanticSpecification
Hegemon.Transaction.Poseidon2V8SemanticAdequacy` from `formal/lean`. Then exact-compare
`lake exe gen_poseidon2_v8_semantic_adequacy_vectors` with
`testdata/formal_core_vectors/poseidon2_v8_semantic_adequacy.json`. The generator pins one fixed
semantic target, the 120/721/43,904 word geometry, all ten semantic families, the two external
action-admission bindings, and the five universal proof obligations. Rust exact-compares those
identities and status values in
`smallwood_poseidon2_v8_semantic_refinement::tests::lean_generated_semantic_adequacy_receipt_matches_rust_status`.
The fixed primitive symbols prevent a receipt from choosing its own hash semantics, but the vector
keeps `exact_primitive_interpretation_refinement_proved=false` until those symbols are proved equal
to the exact Poseidon2 source frames, stablecoin transition, and RFC 7693 BLAKE2b-384 ciphertext
commitment.

For executable typed-lowering coverage, run the focused semantic-refinement unit tests plus the
three exhaustive relation tests for all sixteen single-key masks, every valid approval/final mask,
and disabled/mint/burn stablecoin transitions. Every accepted fixture must round-trip the exact
typed encodings, validate the statement-derived relation context, lower through HGV8RP03, and
replay the verifier-owned adapter. This finite suite is not the universal theorem. Production may
change the generated status to true only after source refinement for the fixed primitive symbols,
an exact decoder for arbitrary accepted packed assignments, and all five Lean refinement
obligations are inhabited; consensus context and exact inline ciphertext checks remain separate
required conjuncts.

The arbitrary-packed forward theorem is now checked separately in the isolated research
package: run `bash scripts/check_formal_crypto.sh` from the repository root. Its credited
`V8Smz9ExactSemanticEndpoint.admitted_packed_project_typed_exact_semantics` root derives
all five unchanged relation conjuncts for the total typed source projection from canonical
public admission and actual generated-program acceptance. This covers arbitrary satisfying
assignments, not only the finite lowering fixtures. The full-action wrapper additionally
requires the actual context and inline-ciphertext matches. The
[endpoint dossier](docs/crypto/smz9-campaign/packed-semantic-endpoint.md) records the
dependency closure and exact premise boundary. Keep the generated production status fields
unchanged until the complete receipt is constructed: the forward result does not prove
reverse lowerer completeness, Rust execution, or proof-byte extraction. The separate
[source-refinement obligations](docs/crypto/smz9-campaign/rust-source-refinement-obligations.md)
pin the reviewed implementation seams without claiming an operational proof.

The same isolated gate now checks the full modeled source-lifetime privacy composition.
`V8Smz9SourceLifetimePrivacy.actual_source_to_public_simulator_bound` derives the three
losses from the actual request factory, its complete byte/error future, and a direct
public-policy simulator. `actual_two_witness_lifetime_bound` requires the same complete
public control-flow erasure and common initial state; witnesses may differ per request.
Each request charges `16,790,291` queries plus its future, including fixed writes.
At analysis indices `q <= 2^65`, `r <= 2^21`, the single-witness bound is `2^-167` and
the two-witness bound is strictly below `2^-128`, conditional on the explicit universal
external reprogramming theorem. Preserve the separate concrete hash/RNG, source/binary,
approved-lifetime and production-authorization boundaries in the
[privacy dossier](docs/crypto/smz9-campaign/privacy-endpoint-completion.md).
